“Ise with you, chappie,” his yellow friend said. The others crowded around. The gramophone stopped and Susy, hugging a bottle, came jerking on her French heels over to the group. She filled the glasses and everybody guzzled gin.

===Chinese===
他们进屋的时候，留声机正往外喷吐一串串黄铜味的爵士音符。苏西跟一个土豆皮色的小子扭来摆去。柯迪小姐跟屋里唯一的黑铁色半蹦半跳。五个小伙子，肤色从棕到黄，坐在那儿喝酒，脸上带着倦怠讥诮的神气。邀泽迪来的那个也在其中。他朝泽迪扬扬手，叫他带着朋友过来。

「坐下，尝尝金酒。」他说。……

泽迪把手往兜里一探，两颗骰子在桌上骨碌碌滚开。

「我跟你们来一把，老伙计。」他那黄皮朋友说。其余的人围拢上来。留声机停了，苏西抱着一瓶酒，踩着法式高跟，一扭一扭地凑到这群人跟前。她把杯子斟满，人人灌起了金酒。

===Original===
Miss Curdy looked the newcomers over, paying particular attention to Jake. A sure-enough eye-filling chocolate, she thought. I would like to make a steady thing of him.

Over by the door two light-brown lads began arguing about an actress of the leading theater of the Black Belt.

“I tell you I knows Gertie Kendall. I know her more’n I know you.”

“Know her mah granny. You knows her just like I do, from the balcony of the Lafayette. Don’t hand me none o’ that fairy stuff, for I ain’t gwine to swallow it.”

===Chinese===
柯迪小姐把新来的几位打量了一遍，格外留意杰克。一个地地道道、叫人眼馋的黑铁色，她暗想。我要把他拴成固定的相好。

门那头，两个浅棕皮的小子为黑人区头牌戏院的一位女演员争了起来。

「我跟你说了，我认得格蒂·肯德尔（Gertie Kendall）。我认得她比认得你还熟。」

「认得她个头。你认得她跟我一个样——在拉斐特剧院（Lafayette）的楼座上认得的。别跟我来那套牛皮，我不咽这一口。」

===Original===
“Youse an aching pain. I knows her, I tell you. I even danced with her at Madame Mulberry’s apartment. You thinks I only hangs out with low-down trash becassin Ise in a place like this, eh? I done met mos’n all our big niggers: Jack Johnson, James Reese Europe, Adah Walker, Buddy, who used to play that theah drum for them Castle Walkers, and Madame Walker.”

“Yaller, it ’pears to me that youse jest a nacherally-born storyteller. You really spec’s me to believe youse been associating with the mucty-mucks of the race? Gwan with you. You’ll be telling me next you done speaks with Charlie Chaplin and John D. Rockefeller—”

Miss Curdy had tuned her ears to the conversation and broke in: “Why, what is that to make so much fuss about? Sure he can dance with Gertie Kendall and know the dickty niggers. In my sporting days I knew Bert Williams and Walker and Adah Overton and Editor Tukslack and all that upstage race gang that wouldn’t touch Jack Johnson with a ten-foot pole. I lived in Washington and had Congressmen for my friends—foop! Why you can get in with the top-crust crowd at any swell ball in Harlem. All you need is clothes and the coin. I know them all, yet I don’t feel a bit haughty mixing here with Susy and you all.”

“I guess you don’t now,” somebody said.

===Chinese===
「你可真是个讨人嫌的祸害。我认得她，跟你说。我甚至在马尔伯里夫人（Madame Mulberry）的公寓里跟她跳过舞。你以为我落到这种地方来，就只配跟下三滥的烂货厮混，啊？咱们的大黑鬼我差不多见全了：杰克·约翰逊（Jack Johnson）、詹姆斯·里斯·欧罗巴（James Reese Europe）、阿达·沃克（Adah Walker）、巴迪（Buddy）——当年就是他给跳城堡舞步（Castle Walk）的那一对打鼓——还有沃克夫人（Madame Walker）。」

「黄皮，依我看，你就是天生一个说书的坯子。你当真指望我相信你跟本族的大人物们厮混过？去你的吧。下回你怕要说你跟卓别林、跟约翰·D·洛克菲勒都攀过话了——」

柯迪小姐早把耳朵支到这场谈话上了，这时候插了进来：「哟，这有什么值得大惊小怪？他当然能跟格蒂·肯德尔跳舞，也认得那些讲究的黑鬼名流。当年我在风月场里的日子，伯特·威廉斯（Bert Williams）、沃克、阿达·奥弗顿（Adah Overton）、图克斯莱克主编（Editor Tukslack），那一帮台面上的种族红人，我都认得——那伙人提起杰克·约翰逊，十英尺的竿子也不肯碰一下。我住在华盛顿，国会议员都是我的朋友——呸！哈莱姆随便哪家阔气的舞会，你想挤进顶层名流那一圈都成。缺的不过是行头和票子。那些人我全认得，可我在这儿跟苏西跟你们大伙儿混，一点儿也不觉得掉价。」

「我看你现在也不觉得。」有人说。

===Original===
Gin went round … and round … and round. … Desultory dancing. … Dice. … Blackjack. … Poker. … The room became a close, live, intense place. Tight-faced, the men seemed interested only in drinking and gaming, while Susy and Miss Curdy, guzzling hard, grew uglier. A jungle atmosphere pervaded the room, and, like shameless wild animals hungry for raw meat, the females savagely searched the eyes of the males. Susy’s eyes always came back to settle upon the lad that had invited Zeddy. He was her real object. And Miss Curdy was ginned up with high hopes of Jake.

Jake threw up the dice and Miss Curdy seized her chance to get him alone for a little while.

“The cards do get so tiresome,” she said. “I wonder how you men can go on and on all night long poking around with poker.”

“Better than worser things,” retorted Jake. Disgusted by the purple streaks, he averted his eyes from the face of the mulattress.

===Chinese===
金酒转过来……转过去……再转过来。……零零散散的舞。……骰子。……二十一点。……扑克。……屋子成了一个闷热、躁动、紧张的地方。男人们绷着脸，好像一心只在喝酒赌钱，苏西和柯迪小姐却越灌越丑。一股丛林气味漫了满屋，那两个雌的像馋生肉的无耻野兽，凶蛮地往男人的眼睛里搜寻。苏西的目光兜来兜去，末了总落回请泽迪来的那个小子身上——那才是她真正的目标。柯迪小姐灌足了酒，对杰克怀着满腹指望。

杰克掷出骰子，柯迪小姐抓住机会，把他单独扯开了一会儿。

「这牌打起来真叫人腻烦，」她说。「真不懂你们男人怎么能一整夜接着一整夜跟扑克戳弄个没完。」

「总比更糟的事强。」杰克顶了回去。那脸上一道道青紫叫他恶心，他把眼睛从这混血女人的脸上挪开。

===Original===
“I don’t know about that,” Miss Curdy bridled. “There’s many nice ways of spending a sociable evening between ladies and gentlemen.”

“Got to show me,” said Jake, simply because the popular phrase intrigued his tongue.

“*And that I can.*”

Irritated, Jake turned to move away.

“Where you going? Scared of a lady?”

===Chinese===
「那可不见得，」柯迪小姐把脸一绷。「淑女跟绅士一道消磨个晚上，雅致的法子多着呢。」

「得让我瞧了才算。」杰克说，不过是那句人人挂在嘴边的口头禅勾了他的舌头。

「*我偏能叫你瞧。*」

杰克来了气，转身要走。

「往哪儿去？怕女人啦？」

===Original===
Jake recoiled from the challenge, and shuffled away from the hideous mulattress. From experience in seaport towns in America, in France, in England, he had concluded that a woman could always go farther than a man in coarseness, depravity, and sheer cupidity. Men were ugly and brutal. But beside women they were merely vicious children. Ignorant about the aim and meaning and fulfillment of life; uncertain and indeterminate; weak. Rude children who loved excelling in spectacular acts to win the applause of women.

But women were so realistic and straight-going. *They* were the real controlling force of life. Jake remembered the bal-musette fights between colored and white soldiers in France. Blacks, browns, yellows, whites. … He remembered the interracial sex skirmishes in England. Men fought, hurt, wounded, killed each other. Women, like blazing torches, egged them on or denounced them. Victims of sex, the men seemed foolish, apelike blunderers in their pools of blood. Didn’t know what they were fighting for, except it was to gratify some vague feeling about women. …

Jake’s thoughts went roaming after his little lost brown of the Baltimore. The difference! She, in one night, had revealed a fine different world to him. Mystery again. A little stray girl. Finer than the finest!

===Chinese===
这一激叫杰克缩了缩，他从那丑陋的混血女人跟前蹭着脱开了。凭他在美洲、法国、英国各个海港的阅历，他早已断定：论粗鄙、论堕落、论赤裸裸的贪婪，女人总归比男人走得远。男人又丑又蛮。可跟女人一比，他们不过是使坏的顽童。不懂得人生的目的、意义和圆成；摇摆、无定、软弱。一群粗野的孩子，专爱在惊世骇俗的营生里逞能，好博得女人们的喝彩。

可女人却是那样现实、那样一往直前。*她们*才是人生真正的掌舵力量。杰克想起法国巴尔-缪塞特（bal-musette）舞厅里有色士兵同白人士兵的斗殴。黑的、棕的、黄的、白的。……他想起英国种种跨种族的风流火拼。男人打啊、伤啊、残啊、杀啊。女人们像烧红的火把，或是撺掇他们，或是唾骂他们。男人做了性欲的牺牲品，倒在血泊里，像一群愚蠢的、猿猴似的瞎撞家伙。不知道自己究竟为什么打，无非是要满足一种对女人的模模糊糊的情绪。……

杰克的思绪游荡开去，去追「巴尔的摩」夜总会里他那位失落的小棕皮。多么不同啊！她只消一夜，就给他显出了一个精妙的、不同的世界。又是神秘。一个流落的小姑娘。比最精妙的还要精妙！

===Original===
Some of the fellows were going. In a vexed spirit, Susy had turned away from her unresponsive mulatto toward Zeddy. Relieved, the mulatto yawned, threw his hands backwards and said: “I guess mah broad is home from Broadway by now. Got to final on home to her. Harlem, lemme see you.”

Miss Curdy was sitting against the mantelpiece, charming Strawberry Lips. Marvellous lips. Salmon-pink and planky. She had hoisted herself upon his knees, her arm around his thick neck.

Jake went over to the mantelpiece to pour a large chaser of beer and Miss Curdy leered at him. She disgusted him. His life was a free coarse thing, but he detested nastiness and ugliness. Guess I’ll haul bottom to Harlem, he thought. Congo Rose was a rearing wild animal, all right, but these women, these boys. … Skunks, tame skunks, all of them!

===Chinese===
有几个家伙要走了。苏西一肚子气，撇下她那位不领情的混血儿，转向了泽迪。混血儿如释重负，打个哈欠，双手往后一搭，说：「我家婆娘这会儿该从百老汇回家了。我得赶紧收场找她去。哈莱姆的各位，回见。」

柯迪小姐靠着壁炉架坐着，迷住「草莓唇」。好一张神奇的嘴。鲑鱼粉红，厚得像板。她把自己架上他的膝盖，一条胳膊搂住他那粗脖子。

杰克走到壁炉架跟前，倒下满满一大杯啤酒解酒，柯迪小姐朝他飞了个媚眼。她叫他恶心。他这一辈子粗放自由，可他嫌恶污秽和丑陋。我看我还是拔脚回哈莱姆吧，他心想。刚果·罗丝（Congo Rose）倒真是一头人立而起的野兽，没错，可这些女人，这些小子……臭鼬，养驯了的臭鼬，全是！

===Original===
He was just going out when a chocolate lad pointed at a light-brown and said: “The pot calls foh four bits, chappie. Come across or stay out.”

“Lemme a quarter!”

“Ain’t got it. Staying out?”

===Chinese===
他正要出门，一个黑铁色小子指着一个浅棕皮的说：「这一锅要五毛，老伙计。要么交钱，要么出局。」

「赊我两毛五！」

「没有。那你出局？」

===Original===
Biff! Square on the mouth. The chocolate leaped up like a tiger-cat at his assailant, carrying over card table, little pile of money, and half-filled gin glasses with a crash. Like an enraged ram goat, he held and butted the light-brown boy twice, straight on the forehead. The victim crumpled with a thud to the floor. Susy jerked over to the felled boy and hauled him, his body leaving a liquid trail, to the door. She flung him out in the corridor and slammed the door.

“Sarves him right, pulling off that crap in mah place. And you, Mis’er Jack Johnson,” she said to the chocolate youth, “lemme miss you quick.”

“He done hits me first,” the chocolate said.

“I knows it, but I ain’t gwina stand foh no roughhouse in mah place. Ise got a dawg heah wif me all ready foh bawking.”

===Chinese===
砰！正打在嘴上。那黑铁色像豹猫一样朝打他的人扑上去，连牌桌、那一小堆票子和半满的金酒杯一齐掀翻，哗啦一片。他像一头暴怒的公山羊，死死抱住那浅棕皮小子，照着脑门直挺挺顶了两下。挨顶的扑通瘫倒在地。苏西一扭一扭地凑到那倒下的小子跟前，把他往门口拖，身子在地上拖出一道水迹。她把他扔到走廊里，砰地摔上了门。

「活该！谁叫他在我屋里撒野耍骰子。还有你，杰克·约翰逊先生，」她对那黑铁色小子说，「快给我走，别叫我再瞧见。」

「是他先动手打我的。」黑铁色说。

「我知道，可我这屋里容不得动粗撒野。我身边养着一条狗，随时预备好要咬人的。」

===Original===
“*K‑hhhhh*, *K‑hhhhh*,” laughed Strawberry Lips. “Oh, boh, I know it’s the trute, but—”

The chocolate lad slunk out of the flat.

“Lavinia,” said Susy to Miss Curdy, “put on that theah ‘Tickling Blues’ on the victroly.”

===Chinese===
「*咳——赫、赫、赫，咳——赫、赫、赫*，」「草莓唇」笑道。「唉，兄弟，我知道这话在理，可是——」

那黑铁色小子灰溜溜地溜出了公寓。

「拉维尼娅，」苏西吩咐柯迪小姐，「把那张《撩人布鲁斯》（Tickling Blues）搁到留声机上去。」

===Original===
The phonograph began its scraping and Miss Curdy started jig-jagging with Strawberry Lips. Jake gloomed with disgust against the door.

“Getting outa this, buddy?” he asked Zeddy.

“Nobody’s chasing *us*, boh.” Zeddy commenced stepping with Susy to the “Tickling Blues.”

===Chinese===
留声机开始沙沙刮擦，柯迪小姐拉着「草莓唇」一颠一簸地跳起来。杰克满心厌恶，阴沉沉地倚在门边。

「不待了，兄弟？」他问泽迪。

「又没人撵*咱们*走，兄弟。」泽迪应了一声，随着《撩人布鲁斯》同苏西踏起了舞步。

===Original===
Outside, Jake found the light-brown boy still half-stunned against the wall.

“Ain’t you gwine at home?” Jake asked him.

“I can’t find a nickel foh car fare,” said the boy.

===Chinese===
外头，杰克看见那浅棕皮小子还半昏迷着，靠在墙上。

「你不回家去？」杰克问他。

「我连五分钱的车钱都凑不出来。」小子说。

===Original===
Jake took him into a saloon and bought him a lemon squash. “Drink that to clear you’ haid,” he said. “And heah’s car fare.” He gave the boy a dollar. “Whar you living at?”

“San Juan Hill.”

“Come on, le’s git the subway, then.”

The Myrtle Avenue Elevated train passed with a high raucous rumble over their heads.

“Myrtle Avenue,” murmured Jake. “Pretty name, all right, but it stinks like a sewer. Legs and feets! Come take me outa it back home to Harlem.”

===Chinese===
杰克把他领进一家酒馆，给他买了一杯柠檬汽水。「喝了，清醒清醒你那脑袋，」他说。「这是车钱。」他给了那小子一块钱。「你住在哪儿？」

「圣胡安山（San Juan Hill）。」

「那好，咱们赶地铁去。」

默特尔大道的高架火车从他们头顶轰隆隆碾过，声音又高又哑。

「默特尔大道，」杰克喃喃道。「名字倒挺好听，可它臭得像条污水沟。大腿和脚丫子！来，带我离开这儿，回哈莱姆老家去。」

## VII Zeddy’s Rise and Fall / VII 泽迪的浮沉

===Original===
Zeddy was scarce in Harlem. And Strawberry Lips was also scarce. It was fully a week after the Myrtle Avenue gin-fest before Jake saw Zeddy again. They met on the pavement in front of Uncle Doc’s saloon.

“Why, where in the sweet name of niggers in Harlem, buddy, you been keeping you’self?”

“Whar you think?”

“Think? I been very much thinking that Nije Gridley done git you.”

“How come you git thataway, boh? Nije Gridley him ain’t got a chawnst on the carve or the draw ag’inst Zeddy Plummer so long as Ise got me a black moon.”

“Well, what’s it done git you, then?”
===Chinese===
泽迪在哈莱姆难得露面了。「草莓唇」（Strawberry Lips）也难得露面了。默特尔大道（Myrtle Avenue）那场杜松子酒宴过去足足一个礼拜，杰克才又见着泽迪。两人在「老杜克」酒馆门前的人行道上碰了头。

「哎，我说，拿哈莱姆黑鬼们甜甜的名号起誓，兄弟，这些天你把自个儿藏哪儿去啦？」

「你说呢？」

「想？我可是结结实实想过，准是尼杰·格里德利（Nije Gridley）把你给收拾了。」

「兄弟，你咋会这么想？只要我脑袋顶上还挂得住这弯黑月亮，尼杰·格里德利那家伙论切牌还是摸牌，都占不去泽迪·普拉默半点儿便宜。」

「那它到底把你咋着啦？」
===Original===
“Myrtle Avenue.”

“Come outa that; you ain’t talking.…”

“The trute as I knows it, buddy.”

“Crazy dog bite mah laig!” cried Jake. “You ain’t telling me that you done gone.…”

“Transfer mah suitcase and all mah pohsitions to Susy.”

“Gin-head Susy!”
===Chinese===
「默特尔大道。」

「拉倒吧，你这话前言不搭后语。……」

「兄弟，就我所知，句句是实。」

「疯狗咬我的腿吧！」杰克叫了起来。「你该不是跟我说，你已经搬了过去……」

「把手提箱连同我全部家当，一并过户给苏西了。」

「『贪杯苏西』！」
===Original===
“Egsactly; that crechur is mah ma-ma now. I done express mahself ovah theah on that very mahv’lously hang-ovah afternoon of that ginnity mawnin’ that you left me theah. And Ise been right theah evah since.”

“Well, Ise got to wish you good luck, buddy, although youse been keeping it so dark.”

“It’s the darkness of new loving, boh. But the honeymoon is good and well ovah, and I’ll be li’l moh in Harlem as usual, looking the chippies and chappies ovah. I ain’t none at all stuck on Brooklyn.”

“It’s a swah hole all right,” said Jake.

“But theah’s sweet stuff in it.” Zeddy tongue-wiped his fleshy lips with a salacious laugh.

“It’s all right, believe me, boh,” he informed Jake. “Susy ain’t nothing to look at like you’ fair-brown queen, but she’s tur’bly sweet loving. You know when a ma-ma ain’t the goods in looks and figure, she’s got to make up foh it some. And that Susy does. And she treats me right. Gimme all I wants to drink and brings home the goodest poke chops and fried chicken foh me to put away under mah shirt… Youse got to come and feed with us all one o’ these heah evenings.”
===Chinese===
「一点儿不错；那家伙如今是我的人儿啦。就在你把我撂在那儿的那个早晨——灌饱了杜松子的早晨——过后那个宿醉得妙不可言的下午，我把心里话跟她挑明了。打那以后我就一直待在那儿。」

「那好，兄弟，我得祝你走运喽，虽说你捂得这么严实。」

「这叫新恋爱的黑灯瞎火，兄弟。不过蜜月是妥妥地过完了，往后我还是照老样子多在哈莱姆转悠，把小妞们、老哥们儿们挨个打量过去。布鲁克林那地方，我压根儿不迷。」

「那地方就是个臭水窟窿，错不了，」杰克说。

「可里头的甜货色少不了。」泽迪淫邪地一笑，舌头把肥厚的嘴唇舔了一圈。

「错不了，信我，兄弟，」他对杰克说。「苏西是不像你那位标致的棕皮女王那么经看，可她那份甜情蜜意，实在得很。你知道，一个娘儿们模样身段要是不顶事，总得想法子找补些回来。苏西就找补上了。她待我又好。我想喝什么就给我什么，还买了顶好的猪排、炸鸡回家，让我往衬衫底下塞个饱……这几个晚上里，你一定得来跟我们搭伙吃一顿。」
===Original===
It was a party of five when Jake went again to Myrtle Avenue for the magnificent free-love feast that Susy had prepared. It was Susy’s free Sunday. Miss Curdy and Strawberry Lips were also celebrating. Susy had concocted a pitcherful of knockout gin cocktails. And such food! Susy could cook. Perhaps it was her splendid style that made her sink all her wages in gin and sweetmen. For she belonged to the ancient aristocracy of black cooks, and knew that she was always sure of a good place, so long as the palates of rich Southerners retained their discriminating taste.

Cream tomato soup. Ragout of chicken giblets. Southern fried chicken. Candied sweet potatoes. Stewed corn. Rum-flavored fruit salad waiting in the icebox.… The stars rolling in Susy’s shining face showed how pleased she was with her art.

She may be fat and ugly as a turkey, thought Jake, but her eats am sure beautiful.

“Heah! Pass me you’ plate,” Susy gave Jake a leg. Zeddy held out his plate again and got a wing. Strawberry Lips received a bit of breast.…
===Chinese===
杰克再去默特尔大道，来赴苏西备下的那桌盛大的自由恋爱筵席时，一共是五个人。那天正是苏西礼拜天歇工的日子。柯迪小姐（Miss Curdy）和草莓唇也来同庆。苏西调制了一大壶上头杜松子鸡尾酒。还有那吃食！苏西真会做菜。也许正因为她这一身好手艺，才让她把工钱统统沉进杜松子酒和小白脸里去。因为她出身黑人厨娘的古老贵族世家，心里有数：只要南方阔佬们那条刁钻的舌头还在挑剔一天，好差事就总有她的份。

奶油番茄汤。鸡杂烩。南方炸鸡。蜜汁红薯。炖玉米。冰柜里还候着朗姆酒味的什锦水果沙拉。……苏西那张亮闪闪的脸上滚着星星点点，看她对自己这门手艺得意成什么样。

她兴许胖得丑得像只火鸡，杰克心想，可她这吃食是真真儿的漂亮。

「喏！把你盘子递过来，」苏西给了杰克一只鸡腿。泽迪又把盘子递上去，得了一只翅膀。草莓唇分到一小块鸡脯。……
===Original===
“No more chicken for me, Susy,” Miss Curdy mumbled, “but I *will* have another helping of that there stewed corn. I don’t know what ingredients yo-all puts in it, but, Lawdy! I never tasted anything near so good.”

Susy beamed and dipped up three spoonfuls of corn. “Plenty, thank you,” Miss Curdy stopped her from filling up her saucer.… Susy drank off a tumbler of cocktail at a draught, and wiped her lips with the white serviette that was stuck into the low neck of her vermillion crepe-de-chine blouse.…

When Jake was ready to leave, Zeddy announced that he would take a little jaunt with him to Harlem.

“You ain’ta gwine to do no sich thing as that,” Susy said.
===Chinese===
「苏西，鸡肉我可再吃不下了，」柯迪小姐嘟哝着，「可那炖玉米，我*非*再来一份不可。你们都搁了些什么作料，我不知道，老天爷呀！这么好的味儿我可从来没沾过。」

苏西满脸堆笑，舀了三大勺玉米。「够了够了，谢谢，」柯迪小姐拦住她，没让她把小碟添满。……苏西一仰脖灌下一玻璃杯鸡尾酒，用餐巾纸擦了擦嘴唇——那条白餐巾掖在她朱红色双绉低领罩衫的领口里。……

杰克起身要走的时候，泽迪宣布，他要跟杰克一道去哈莱姆小遛一趟。

「这种事你可休想干出来，」苏西说。
===Original===
“Yes I is,” responded Zeddy. “Wha’ there is to stop me?”

“I is,” said Susy.

“And what foh?”

“’Causen I don’t wanchu to go to Harlem. What makes you niggers love Harlem so much? Because it’s a bloody ungodly place where niggers nevah go to bed. All night running around speakeasies and cabarets, where bad, hell-bent nigger womens am giving up themselves to open sin.”
===Chinese===
「我偏要去，」泽迪应道。「有谁拦得住我？」

「我拦，」苏西说。

「凭什么？」

「就凭我不要你去哈莱姆。你们这帮黑鬼咋就那么爱哈莱姆？就因为那是个该死的、不敬神的地方，黑鬼们从来不睡觉。整宿整宿围着地下酒吧和夜场乱转，那些坏透的、一门心思奔地狱的黑鬼娘们儿，把自个儿明晃晃地往罪孽里送。」
===Original===
Susy stood broad and aggressive against the window overlooking Myrtle Avenue.

“Harlem is all right,” said Zeddy. “I ain’t knocking round no cabarets and speakeasies. Ahm just gwine ovah wif Jake to see somathem boys.”

“Can that boy business!” cried Susy. “I’ve had anuff hell scrapping wif the women ovah mah mens. I ain’t agwine to have no Harlem boys seducin’ mah man away fwom me. The boy business is a fine excuse indeedy foh sich womens as ain’t wise. I always heah the boss say to the missus, ‘I gwine out foh a little time wif the boys, dearie,’ when him wants an excuse foh a night off. I ain’t born yestiday, honey. If you wants the boys foh a li’l game o’ poky, you bring ’em ovah heah. I ain’t got the teeeniest bit of objection, and Ise got plenty o’ good Gordon Dry foh eve’body.”

“Ise got to go scares them up to bring them heah,” said Zeddy.
===Chinese===
苏西宽宽地堵在那扇俯望默特尔大道的窗户跟前，气势汹汹。

「哈莱姆没什么不好，」泽迪说。「我又不打算去夜场、地下酒吧里瞎晃。我就是跟杰克过去瞧瞧几个哥儿们。」

「少拿哥儿们那套搪塞！」苏西嚷道。「为了我这几个男人，我跟娘们儿们撕破脸的架吵得够够的了。我可不许什么哈莱姆的哥儿们把我的男人从我身边勾走。哥儿们那一套，对那些不懂事的婆娘们来说，倒真是顶体面的借口。我总听东家跟太太说：『亲爱的，我出去一小会儿，跟哥儿几个聚聚。』——凡是他想偷一晚上的闲，都拿这话搪塞。宝贝儿，我又不是昨天才生的。你要真想跟哥儿们小来一局扑克，就把他们带到我这儿来。我一丁点儿意见都没有，上好的戈登干杜松子酒（Gordon Dry）管够，人人有份。」

「那我总得先去把他们惊动起来，才能带到你这儿呀，」泽迪说。
===Original===
“But not tonight or no night,” declared Susy. “You kain do that in the daytime, foh you ain’t got nothing to do.”

Zeddy moved toward the mantelpiece to get his cap, but Susy blocked his way and held the cap behind her.

Zeddy looked savagely in her eyes and growled: “Come outa that, sistah, and gimme mah cap. It ain’t no use stahting trouble.”

Susy looked steadily in his eyes and chucked the cap at him. “Theah’s you cap, but ef you stahts leaving me nights you…”

“What will you do?” asked Zeddy.
===Chinese===
「今儿晚上不行，哪个晚上都不行，」苏西一口咬定。「要找人只能白天去，白天你横竖闲得没事干。」

泽迪朝壁炉台挪过去拿他的帽子，苏西拦住他的道，把帽子背在身后。

泽迪眼睛里冒着凶光，低吼道：「拉倒吧，大姐，把帽子还我。惹是生非没好处。」

苏西眼睛直直地盯着他，把帽子朝他一扔。「帽子还你。可你要是敢开头上晚上往外溜，那你就……」

「你能把我咋样？」泽迪问。
===Original===
“I’ll put you’ block in the street.”

Zeddy’s countenance fell flat from its high aggressiveness.

“Well s’long, eve’body,” said Jake.

Zeddy put on his cap and rocked out of the apartment after him. In the street he asked Jake, “Think I ought to take a crack at Harlem with you tonight, boh?”

“Not ef you loves you’ new home, buddy,” Jake replied.
===Chinese===
「我就把你那摊家当扔到大街上去。」

泽迪那张正凶得冒烟的脸，登时塌了下去。

「得，各位回见，」杰克说。

泽迪扣上帽子，摇摇摆摆跟着他出了公寓。到了街上，他问杰克：「兄弟，你说我今晚该不该跟你去哈莱姆闯一闯？」

「要是你还疼你那个新窝，就别去，兄弟，」杰克答道。
===Original===
“Bull! That plug-ugly black woman is ornery like hell. I ain’t gwineta let her bridle and ride me.… You ain’t in no pickle like that with Rose, is you?”

“Lawd, no! I do as I wanta. But I’m one independent cuss, buddy. We ain’t sitchuate the same. I works.”

“Black womens when theyse ugly am all sistahs of Satan,” declared Zeddy.

“It ain’t the black ones only,” said Jake.

“I wish I could hit things off like you, boh,” said Zeddy.… “Well, I’ll see you all some night at Billy Biasse’s joint… S’long. Don’t pick up no bad change.”
===Chinese===
「放屁！那黑婆娘丑得像根木桩，还贱得冒烟。我决不能让她套上笼头骑着走。……你跟罗丝（Rose）总没落到这步田地吧，啊？」

「老天，没有！我爱咋咋地。可我是个独立的种，兄弟。咱俩处境不一样。我是干活吃饭的人。」

「黑皮娘们儿只要一丑，个个都是撒旦的姊妹，」泽迪断言。

「丑的可不光是黑皮的，」杰克说。

「真巴不得我也能混得像你这样顺当，兄弟，」泽迪说。……「得，哪天晚上比利·比亚斯（Billy Biasse）的场子上见。……回见。别沾上什么烂晦气。」
===Original===
From that evening Zeddy began to discover that it wasn’t all fine and lovely to live sweet. Formerly he had always been envious when any of his pals pointed out an extravagantly-dressed dark dandy and remarked, “He was living sweet.” There was something so romantic about the sweet life. To be the adored of a Negro lady of means, or of a pseudo grass-widow whose husband worked on the railroad, or of a hardworking laundress or cook. It was much more respectable and enviable to be sweet—to belong to the exotic aristocracy of sweetmen than to be just a common tout.

But there were strings to Susy’s largesse. The enjoyment of Harlem’s low night life was prohibited to Zeddy. Susy was jealous of him in the proprietary sense. She believed in free love all right, but not for the man she possessed and supported. She warned him against the ornery hussies of her race.

“Nigger hussies nevah wanta git next to a man ’cep’n’ when he’s a-looking good to another woman,” Susy declared. “I done gived you fair warning to jest keep away from the buffet-flat widdahs and thim Harlem street floaters; foh ef I ketch you making a fool woman of me, I’ll throw you’ pants in the street.”
===Chinese===
打那天晚上起，泽迪渐渐咂摸出来：过甜日子，并不全是美满可爱的事。从前，弟兄们里头谁指着个穿得花里胡哨的黑皮纨绔说一句「这位过着甜日子」，他总要眼热半天。甜日子里头自有一股浪漫劲儿。让一位有家私的黑人太太宠着，或是让丈夫在铁路上做工的「活寡妇」宠着，再不然，让一个卖死力气的洗衣妇、厨娘宠着。当个甜人儿——跻身小白脸那异国风味的贵族行列——可比当个寻常拉客的闲汉体面得多，也惹人艳羡得多。

可苏西的恩典是拴着绳儿的。哈莱姆下等的夜生活，泽迪一概禁足。苏西的醋劲是东家式的。自由恋爱她信奉得很，可轮到她占有并供养的男人，就另当别论了。她警告他，叫他从自家种族里那些下贱婆娘身边走远点儿。

「黑鬼婆娘们从来不想跟一个男人亲近，除非别的女人正瞅着他顺眼，」苏西放话。「我把丑话跟你交代明白：私酒公寓里的那些寡妇，还有哈莱姆街头那些游荡的野鸡，你都给我躲远点儿；叫我逮着你拿我当个傻娘们儿耍，我就把你裤子扔到大街上去。」
===Original===
“Hi, but youse talking sistah. Why don’t you wait till you see something before you staht in chewing the rag?”

“I done give you the straight stuff in time so you kain watch you’self when I kain’t watch you. I ain’t bohding and lodging no black man foh’m to be any other nigger woman’s daddy.”

So, in a few pointed phrases, Susy let Zeddy understand precisely what she would stand for. Zeddy was well kept like a prince of his type. He could not complain about food… and bed. Susy was splendid in her matriarchal way, rolling her eyes with love or disapproval at him, according to the exigencies of the moment.

The Saturday-night gin parties went on as usual. The brown and high-brown boys came and swilled. Miss Curdy was a constant visitor, frequently toting Strawberry Lips along. About her general way of handling things Susy brooked no criticism from Zeddy. She had bargained with him in the interest of necessity and of rivalry and she paid and paid fully, but grimly. She was proud to have a man to boss about in an intimate, casual way.
===Chinese===
「嚯，大姐，你这话说得可够冲。咋不等瞧见了真凭实据，再开牙嚼舌头？」

「我这是赶早把实底儿交给你，好叫我看不住你的时候，你自己看住自个儿。我管吃管住养个黑男人，可不是放他去给别的黑鬼娘们儿当爹地的。」

就这么几句带尖儿带刺的话，苏西叫泽迪明明白白知道了她的章程。泽迪被供养得妥妥帖帖，俨然他那一类里的王子。吃食……和床笫，他都挑不出半个字。苏西母系家长派头十足，朝他眼波一转，或是含情，或是嗔怪，全看当下的情势。

礼拜六晚上的杜松子酒会照旧开场。棕皮的、上等棕皮的哥儿们来了就海灌。柯迪小姐是雷打不动的常客，动不动还捎上草莓唇。苏西当家理信用的大章程，泽迪说半个不字也不行。出于不得已，也出于争风头的需要，她跟泽迪做过交易；她付账，付得分文不短，却付得铁面无情。身边有个男人，可以亲亲热热、随随便便地由她使唤，这是她的骄傲。
===Original===
“Git out another bottle of gin, Zeddy.…”

“Bring along that packet o’ saltines.…”

“Put on that theah ‘Tickling Blues’ that we’s all just crazy about.”

To have an aggressive type like Zeddy at her beck and call considerably increased Susy’s prestige and clucking pride. She noticed, with carefully-concealed delight, that the interest of the yellow gin-swillers was piqued. She became flirtatious and coy by turns. And she was rewarded by fresh attentions. Even Miss Curdy was now meeting with new adventures, and she was prompted to expatiate upon men and love to Susy.
===Chinese===
「再开一瓶杜松子酒来，泽迪。……」

「把那包苏打饼干也捎来。……」

「把那张《撩心布鲁斯》（Tickling Blues）放上去，咱们个个都爱疯了的。」

有泽迪这样一尊凶神招之即来、挥之即去，苏西的威望和她那老母鸡咯咯式的得意劲儿，见风就长。她留心到，那些黄皮灌酒客的兴趣被勾起来了，心里乐开了花，面上却藏得严严实实。她一会儿卖弄风情，一会儿又扭捏作态。果然换来了新的殷勤。连柯迪小姐如今也遇上了新的艳遇，于是来了兴致，跟苏西大谈男人和爱情。
===Original===
“Men’s got a whole lot of women in their nature, I tell you. Just as women never really see a man until he’s looking good to another woman and the hussies want to steal him, it’s the same thing with men, mah dear. So soon as a woman is all sugar and candy for another man, you find a lot of them heartbreakers all trying to get next to her. Like a set of strutting game cocks all priming themselves to crow over a li’l piece o’ nothing.”

“That’s the gospel trute indeed,” agreed Susy. “I done have a mess of knowledge ’bout men tucked away heah.” Susy tapped her head of tight-rolled kinks knotted with scraps of ribbon of different colors. “I pays foh what I know and I’ve nevah been sorry, either. Yes, mam, I done larned about mah own self fust. Had no allusions about mahself. I knowed that I was black and ugly and no-class and unejucated. And I knowed that I was bohn foh love.… Mah mammy did useter warn me about love. All what the white folks call white slavery theseadays. I dunno ef theah’s another name foh the nigger-an’-white side ovit down home in Dixie. Well, I soon found out it wasn’t womens alone in the business, sposing thimselves like vigitables foh sale in the market. No, mam! I done soon l’arned that the mens was most buyable thimselves. Mah heartbreaking high-yaller done left me sence—how miny wintahs I been counting this heah Nothan snow? All thim and some moh—dawggone ef I remimber. But evah since I been paying sistah, paying good and hard foh mah loving feelings.”

“Life ain’t no country picnic with sweet flute and fiddle,” Miss Curdy sighed.

“Indeed not,” Susy was emphatic. “It ain’t got nothing to do with the rubbish we l’arn at Sunday school and the sweet snooziness I used to lap up in thim blue-cover story books. My God! the things I’ve seen! Working with white folks, so dickty and high-and-mighty, you think theyse nevah oncet naked and thim feets nevah touch ground. Yet all the silks and furs and shining diamonds can’t hide the misery a them lives.… Servants and heartbreakers from outside stealing the husband’s stuff. And all the men them that can’t find no sweet-loving life at home. Lavinia, I done seen life.”
===Chinese===
「男人的天性里就窝着一整个后宫的娘们儿，我跟你说。女人家从来真瞅不见一个男人——要等别的女人瞅他顺眼了、贱货们动手想偷了，这才瞅得见；男人也是一路货，亲爱的。哪个女人刚对另一个男人甜得跟糖似的，你瞧吧，一帮拆心郎立马全围上来了，个个都想凑到跟前。活像一群昂首阔步的斗鸡，个个梳理停当，要为丁点儿大的空名打鸣称雄。」

「这话可是不折不扣的福音真理，」苏西附和。「关于男人的学问，我这儿存着一大堆呢。」苏西敲了敲脑袋——紧紧盘卷的鬈发上，各色碎布条扎成一个个结。「我这些见识都是花真金白银买来的，我也从不后悔。可不，太太，我头一个认清的就是我自己。对自个儿没存半点幻想。我知道自己又黑又丑、没身份、没受过教育。可我也知道自己生来就是为着爱。……我老娘从前老拿爱这个字敲打我。就是白佬们如今嘴里的『白奴』那一套。这黑鬼掺白佬的营生，在老家迪克西那头有没有别的名堂，我就不知道了。反正我不多久就咂摸出来了：这一行里头可不止娘们儿，把自个儿摆得跟菜市上待售的青菜萝卜似的。哪里的话，太太！我不多久就学乖了：男人自个儿才是顶经不起买的。我那位让我伤透了心的上等黄皮，甩下我走啦——这北方的雪，我数着过了几个冬天啦？全数上还得添几个——狗娘养的，我记不清喽。反正打那以后我就一直在付账，大姐，为了我这一点痴情，付得又足又狠。」

「日子可不是乡下的野餐会，还有甜滋滋的笛子提琴伺候着，」柯迪小姐叹道。

「可不是嘛，」苏西斩钉截铁。「那跟主日学校教给咱们的那些烂道理不沾边儿，跟我从前从那几本蓝皮故事书里舔进肚的甜腻腻的迷魂汤也不沾边儿。我的老天爷！我可是开过眼的！在白佬家里做工，他们一个个讲究人似的、高高在上，你当他们从来没赤条条过一回，那双脚从来没沾过地。可绸缎、皮草、亮闪闪的钻石，全遮不住他们日子里的苦水。……外头进来的仆人和拆心郎，偷丈夫的家当。还有那些男人，在家里寻不着一点甜情蜜意。拉维尼娅（Lavinia），我是把日子看透啦。」
===Original===
“Me, too, I have seen the real life, mixing as I used to in *real* society,” said Miss Curdy.

“I know society, too, honey, even though I only knows it watching from the servant window. And I know it ain’t no different from *us*. It’s the same life even ef they drink champagne and we drink gin.”

“You said it and said it right,” responded Miss Curdy.
===Chinese===
「我也见过真正的生活，想当年我出入的可是*正牌*上流社会，」柯迪小姐说。

「上流社会我也懂得，宝贝儿，虽说我是打佣人的小窗户里瞧会的。我瞧明白了，那玩意儿跟*咱们*没什么两样。一样的日子，哪怕他们喝香槟、咱们喝杜松子。」

「你说得对，说得句句在理，」柯迪小姐应道。
===Original===
---

Zeddy discovered that in his own circles in Harlem he had become something of a joke. It was known that he was living sweet. But his buddies talked about his lady riding him with a cruel bit.

“He was kept, all right,” they said, “kept under ‘Gin-head’ Susy’s skirt.”

He had had to fight a fellow in Dixie Red’s poolroom, for calling him a “skirt-man.”

He was even teased by Billy Biasse or Billy, the Wolf, as he was nicknamed. Billy boasted frankly that he had no time for women. Black women, or the whole diversified world of the sex were all the same to him.
===Chinese===
---

泽迪发觉，在哈莱姆他自己那个圈子里，他已成了半桩笑料。他过甜日子，人尽皆知。可弟兄们背地里说，他家娘子给他勒上一副狠嚼子骑着走。

「他让人养着，错不了，」他们说，「养在『贪杯苏西』的裙子底下。」

在「迪克西·雷德」（Dixie Red）弹子房，他跟一个家伙动过一回手，就因为那人管他叫「裙下汉」。

就连比利·比亚斯——人称「狼」比利（Billy the Wolf）——也拿他打趣。比利直言不讳地夸口：他没工夫搭理女人。黑人女人也罢，整个五花八门的粉黛世界也罢，在他眼里统统一个样。
===Original===
“So Harlem, after the sun done set, has no fun at all foh you, eh, boh?” Billy asked Zeddy.

Zeddy growled something indistinct.

“Sweet with the bit in you’ mouf. Black woman riding her nigger. Great life, boh, ef you don’t weaken.”

“Bull! Wha’s the matter with you niggers, anyhow?” Zeddy said in a sort of general way. “Ain’t it better than being a wolf?”

“Ise a wolf, all right, but I ain’t a lone one,” Billy grinned. “I guess Ise the happiest, well-feddest wolf in Harlem. Oh, boy!”
===Chinese===
「这么说，日头一落山，哈莱姆对你就再没半点儿乐子啦，啊，兄弟？」比利问泽迪。

泽迪嘟哝了一声，含混不清。

「甜日子过着，嘴里还勒着嚼子。黑女人骑着她家黑鬼。美日子啊，兄弟——只要你自己不先瘫软。」

「放屁！你们这帮黑鬼到底哪根筋不对？」泽迪泛泛地回了一句。「总比当狼强吧？」

「狼我是当定了，可不是孤零零一头，」比利咧嘴一笑。「我估摸我是哈莱姆最快活、喂养得最肥的一头狼。哦，好小子！」
===Original===
Zeddy spent that evening in Harlem drinking with Jake and two more longshoremen at Uncle Doc’s saloon. Late in the night they went to the Congo. Zeddy returned to Myrtle Avenue, an hour before it was time for Susy to rise, fully ginned up.

To Susy’s “Whar you been?” he answered, “Shut up or I’ll choke you,” staggered, swayed, and swept from the dresser a vase of chrysanthemums that broke on the floor.

“Goddam fool flowers,” he growled. “Why in hell didn’t you put them out of the way, hey, you Suze?”

“Oh, keep quiet and come along to bed,” said Susy.

A week later he repeated the performance, coming home with alarming symptoms of gin hiccup. Susy said nothing. After that Zeddy began to prance, as much as a short, heavy-made human could, with the bit out of his mouth.…
===Chinese===
那天晚上，泽迪是在哈莱姆过的，同杰克和另外两个码头装卸工在「老杜克」酒馆喝酒。夜深了，他们又去了「刚果」（the Congo）。泽迪回到默特尔大道时，离苏西起身还有一个钟头，杜松子酒灌得十成十。

苏西问「你上哪儿去了？」他回一句「闭嘴，不然掐死你」，跌跌撞撞、摇摇晃晃，一胳膊扫掉了五斗柜上那瓶菊花，花瓶摔碎在地板上。

「该死的破花，」他咆哮。「你咋不把它们搁到不碍事的地方去，啊，你说你，苏西？」

「哎，少废话，上床睡觉，」苏西说。

一个礼拜后他又来了一回，到家时杜松子酒的酒嗝打得吓人。苏西一声没吭。打那以后，泽迪开始撒欢儿了——就他那又矮又沉的身板，能撒到什么份儿上就撒到什么份儿上——嚼子也摘了。……
===Original===
One Saturday night Susy’s gin party was a sad failure. Nobody came beside Miss Curdy with Strawberry Lips. (Zeddy had left for Harlem in the afternoon.) They drank to themselves and played coon-can. Near midnight, when Miss Curdy was going, she said offhandedly, “I wouldn’t mind sampling one of those Harlem cabarets now.” Susy at once seized upon the idea.

“Sure. Let’s go to Harlem for a change.”

They caught the subway train for Harlem. Arrived there they gravitated to the Congo.

Before Susy left Myrtle Avenue, Zeddy was already at the Congo with a sweet, timid, satin-faced brown just from down home, that he had found at Aunt Hattie’s and induced to go with him to the cabaret. Jake sat at Zeddy’s table. Zeddy was determined to go the limit of independence, to show the boys that he was a cocky sweetman and no skirt-man. Plenty of money. He was treating. He wore an elegant nigger-brown sports suit and patent-leather shoes with cream-light spats such as all the sweet swells love to strut in. If Zeddy had only been taller, trimmer, and well-arched he would have been one of Harlem’s dandiest sports.
===Chinese===
一个礼拜六晚上，苏西的杜松子酒会办得冷清凄惨。除了柯迪小姐带着草莓唇，再没旁人来。（泽迪下午就动身去哈莱姆了。）她们自己给自己斟酒，打孔坎牌（coon-can）。快到半夜，柯迪小姐临走时随口说了一句：「这会儿去哈莱姆哪家夜场开开眼，我倒不嫌弃。」苏西立马逮住了这个主意。

「可不是。咱们去哈莱姆换换口味。」

她们赶上开往哈莱姆的地铁。一到那儿，脚下不由自主就吸向了「刚果」。

苏西还没离开默特尔大道，泽迪已经坐在「刚果」了，身边带着一个刚打老家来的甜妞儿——腼腆怕生，一张缎子面儿的棕皮脸——是他在哈蒂大婶的馆子里相中的，好说歹说才哄得她跟他一块儿来夜场。杰克坐在泽迪这一桌。泽迪打定主意，要把独立这面旗打到极限，好叫弟兄们瞧瞧：他是神气活现的小白脸，不是什么裙下汉。钱管够。他请客。他一身讲究的黑鬼棕运动装，漆皮鞋配奶油白的鞋罩，正是甜日子里的阔少们最爱穿着四处显摆的行头。可惜泽迪个子要是再高些、身板再利落些、脚背再漂亮些，他本可以挤进哈莱姆顶体面的花花公子之列。
===Original===
His newfound brown had a glass of Virginia Dare before her; he was drinking gin. Jake, Scotch-and-soda; and Rose, who sat with them when she was not entertaining, had ordered White Rock. The night before, or rather the early morning after her job was done, she had gone on a champagne party and now she was sobering up.

Billy Biasse was there at a neighboring table with a longshoreman and a straw-colored boy who was a striking advertisement of the Ambrozine Palace of Beauty. The boy was made up with high-brown powder, his eyebrows were elongated and blackened up, his lips streaked with the dark rouge so popular in Harlem, and his carefully-straightened hair lay plastered and glossy under Madame Walker’s absinthe-colored salve “for milady of fashion and color.”

“Who’s the doll baby at the Wolf’s table?” Zeddy asked.
===Chinese===
新相中的棕皮妞儿面前摆着一杯弗吉尼亚·戴尔（Virginia Dare）葡萄酒；他喝杜松子酒。杰克喝苏打兑威士忌；罗丝——不登台陪客的时候就坐他们这一桌——要了白岩（White Rock）汽水。头天夜里，说得确切些，是她活儿干完之后的那个凌晨，她跟人闹了一场香槟酒会，这会儿才慢慢醒过酒来。

比利·比亚斯就在邻桌，带着一个码头装卸工，还有一个草黄脸皮的哥儿——活脱脱是「安布罗辛美人宫」（Ambrozine Palace of Beauty）的一幅扎眼活广告。那哥儿扑了上等棕色的粉，眉毛拉得长长的、描得黑黑的，嘴唇抹了哈莱姆顶时兴的暗红胭脂，精心拉直的头发在沃克夫人（Madame Walker）苦艾酒色的发膏底下服服帖帖、油光水滑——那膏子号称「献给摩登的有色淑女」。

「『狼』那一桌上那个小宝贝儿是谁？」泽迪问。
===Original===
“Tha’s mah dancing pardner,” Rose answered.

“Another entertainer? The Congo is gwine along fast enough.”

“You bet you,” said Jake. “And the ofays will soon be nosing it out. Then we’ll have to take a back seat.”

“Who’s the Wolf?” Timidly Zeddy’s girl asked.

Zeddy pointed out Billy.
===Chinese===
「那是我的舞伴儿，」罗丝答。

「又添一位卖艺的？『刚果』这买卖蹿得可够快的。」

「那还用说，」杰克说。「白佬们很快就会嗅着味儿摸过来。到那时，咱们就只好往后排坐啦。」

「『狼』是谁呀？」泽迪的妞儿怯生生地问。

泽迪指了指比利。
===Original===
“But why Wolf?”

“*K-hhhhhhh*—*K-hhhhhhhh*…” Zeddy laughed. “ ’Causen he eats his own kind.”

It was time for Rose to dance. Her partner had preceded her to the open space and was standing, arm akimbo against the piano, talking to the pianist. The pianist was a slight-built, long-headed fellow. His face shone like anthracite, his eyes were arresting, intense, deep-yellow slits. He seemed in a continual state of swaying excitement, whether or not he was playing.

> When Luty dances, everything
> Is dancing in the cabaret.
> The second fiddle asks the first:
> What makes you sound that funny way?
> The drum talks in so sweet a voice,
> The cymbal answers in surprise,
> The lights put on a brighter glow
> To match the shine of Luty’s eyes.

> For he’s a foot-manipulating fool
> When he hears that crazy moan
> Come rolling, rolling outa that saxophone.…
> Watch that strut; there’s no keeping him cool
> When he’s a-rearing with that saxophone.…
> Oh, the tearing, tantalizing tone!
> Of that moaning saxophone.…
> That saxophone.…
> That saxophone.…
> That saxophone.…
===Chinese===
「可为啥叫『狼』？」

「*咳——嗬嗬嗬！咳——嗬嗬嗬嗬！*……」泽迪笑了。「因为他专吃自家同类。」

轮到罗丝跳舞了。她的舞伴先一步到了场子中间，一手叉腰，胳膊肘支在钢琴上，跟琴师搭话。琴师是个瘦小个子、脑袋窄长的家伙。一张脸亮得像无烟煤，一双眼睛勾人魂魄，灼灼的，是两道深黄的细缝。不管手上弹不弹琴，他似乎永远泡在一种摇摇荡荡的兴奋里。

> 露蒂（Luty）一开舞，夜场里
> 万物都跟着舞成一片。
> 二提琴问一提琴：
> 你那调门咋这般逗趣？
> 鼓儿开口，嗓音甜，
> 铜钹吃惊地把话搭，
> 灯火又添几分亮，
> 好配露蒂眼里的光华。

> 只因他一听那疯魔的呻吟
> 滚滚、滚滚涌出那管萨克斯风……
> 就成了个拿脚板变戏法的痴人；
> 瞧那趟子步，休想叫他冷下来，
> 他正和着萨克斯风直往上耸……
> 哦，那撕人的、撩人的调门！
> 那声声呜咽的萨克斯风……
> 那萨克斯风……
> 那萨克斯风……
> 那萨克斯风……
===Original===
They danced, Rose and the boy. Oh, they danced! An exercise of rhythmical exactness for two. There was no motion she made that he did not imitate. They reared and pranced together, smacking palm against palm, working knee between knee, grinning with real joy. They shimmied, breast to breast, bent themselves far back and shimmied again. Lifting high her short skirt and showing her green bloomers, Rose kicked. And in his tight nigger-brown suit, the boy kicked even with her. They were right there together, neither going beyond the other.…

And the pianist! At intervals his yellow eyes, almost bloodshot, swept the cabaret with a triumphant glow, gave the dancers a caressing look, and returned to the ceiling. Lean, smart fingers beating barbaric beauty out of a white frame. Brown bodies, caught up in the wild rhythm, wiggling and swaying in their seats.

> For he’s a foot-manipulating fool
> When he hears that crazy moan
> Come rolling, rolling outa that saxophone.…
> That saxophone.…
> That saxophone.…
===Chinese===
他们跳起来了，罗丝和那哥儿。哦，跳起来了！两个人的韵律严丝合缝，齐得像一门功课。她没有哪个动作是他不跟着学的。他们一道腾跃、一道欢蹦，掌心拍着掌心，膝盖别着膝盖，咧开嘴笑得真心实意。他们跳希米舞，胸脯贴着胸脯，身子往后仰到极处，又接着扭。罗丝把短裙高高撩起，露出绿灯笼裤，踢腿。那哥儿穿着紧绷绷的黑鬼棕套装，踢得跟她分毫不差。两人严丝合缝地在一处，谁也不越过谁半分。……

还有那琴师！他那一双黄眼睛，红丝几乎布满，隔一阵子便扫过整个夜场，闪着得意的光，朝舞者抚爱地一瞥，又回到天花板上去。精瘦利落的手指，从一架白琴的框子里敲打出野性的美。一具具棕色的身子，叫那狂放的节奏攫住了，在座位上扭摆、摇晃。

> 只因他一听那疯魔的呻吟
> 滚滚、滚滚涌出那管萨克斯风……
> 就成了个拿脚板变戏法的痴人；
> 那萨克斯风……
> 那萨克斯风……
===Original===
Rose was sipping her White Rock. Her partner, at Billy’s table, sucked his iced crème de menthe through a straw. The high wave of joyful excitement had subsided and the customers sat casually drinking and gossiping as if they had not been soaring a minute before in a realm of pure joy.

From his place, giving a good view of the staircase, Zeddy saw two apparently familiar long legs swinging down the steps. Sure enough, he knew those big, thick-soled red boots.

“Them feets look jest laka Strawberry Lips’ own,” he said to Jake. Jake looked and saw first Strawberry Lips enter the cabaret, with Susy behind balancing upon her French heels, and Miss Curdy. Susy was gorgeous in a fur coat of rich shiny black, like her complexion. Opened, it showed a cerise blouse and a yellow-and-mauve check skirt. Her head of thoroughly-straightened hair flaunted a green hat with a decoration of red ostrich plumes.

“Great balls of fire! Here’s you doom, buddy,” said Jake.
===Chinese===
罗丝小口啜着她的白岩汽水。她的舞伴在比利那桌，正拿吸管嘬他那杯冰薄荷甜酒。欢快兴奋的高浪头退下去了，客人们随意地喝着、闲磕牙，好像一分钟之前他们并没有在一个纯粹欢愉的天地里翱翔过似的。

泽迪坐的位子正望得见楼梯。他瞧见两条显然眼熟的长腿，一荡一荡下了台阶。错不了，那双厚底大红靴子他认得。

「那双脚瞅着活像草莓唇自个儿的，」他对杰克说。杰克一望，先看见草莓唇进了夜场，苏西跟在后头，法国高跟儿上稳稳地端着架子，柯迪小姐押后。苏西一身华贵：一件名贵的油亮乌黑大氅，跟她那肤色一个色。敞开来，露出一件樱桃红的罩衫、一条黄紫相间的格纹裙。她那头拉得笔直的头发上，神气地晃着一顶绿帽子，缀着红鸵鸟翎毛的花饰。

「老天爷放火球啦！你的大限到啦，兄弟，」杰克说。
===Original===
“Doom, mah granny,” retorted Zeddy. “Ef that theah black ole cow come fooling near me tonight, I’ll show her who’s wearing the pants.”

Susy did not see Zeddy until her party was seated. It was Miss Curdy who saw him first. She dug into Susy’s side with her elbow and cried:

“For the love of Gawd, looka there!”

Susy’s star eyes followed Miss Curdy’s. She glared at Zeddy and fixed her eyes on the girl with him for a moment. Then she looked away and grunted: “He thinks he’s acting smart, eh? Him and I will wrastle that out to a salution, but I ain’t agwine to raise no stink in heah.”

“He’s got some more nerve pulling off that low-down stuff, and on your money, too,” said Miss Curdy.
===Chinese===
「什么大限，去他奶奶的，」泽迪回敬。「那头黑老母牛今晚敢上我跟前来晃，我就叫她瞧瞧，这家里裤子到底穿在谁腿上。」

直到她们一伙坐定了，苏西才看见泽迪。先瞧见他的是柯迪小姐。她一胳膊肘捅进苏西的腰眼，叫了起来：

「看在老天爷的份上，快往那儿瞧！」

苏西那双星星眼顺着柯迪小姐的目光跟了过去。她狠狠瞪住泽迪，又把眼钉在他身旁那姑娘身上，钉了一会儿。这才把目光挪开，哼了一声：「他当自个儿挺能耐是吧？他跟我这笔账，总得摔跤摔出个结果来。可我不会在这儿撒泼惹臭。」

「他这胆子可真够肥，干得出这种下三滥的勾当，花的还是你的钱，」柯迪小姐说。
===Original===
“Who that?” asked Strawberry Lips.

“Ain’t you seen your best friends over there?” retorted Miss Curdy.

Strawberry Lips waved at Zeddy and Jake, but they were deliberately keeping their eyes away from Susy’s table. He got up to go to them.

“Where you going?” Miss Curdy asked.

“To chin wif—”
===Chinese===
「那是谁呀？」草莓唇问。

「你没瞧见你那几位顶好的朋友在那头吗？」柯迪小姐抢白道。

草莓唇朝泽迪和杰克招手，可他俩存心把眼睛躲着苏西那桌。他起身要走过去。

「上哪儿去？」柯迪小姐问。

「去跟——唠几句——」
===Original===
A yell startled the cabaret. A girl had slapped another’s face and replied to her victim’s cry of pain with, “If you no like it you can lump it!”

“You low an’ dutty bobbin-bitch!”

“Bitch is bobbin in you’ sistah’s coffin.”

They were West Indian girls.

“I’ll mek mah breddah beat you’ bottom foh you.”
===Chinese===
一声尖叫惊动了整个夜场。一个姑娘掴了另一个一记耳光，听着对方疼得叫出声，她回敬道：「不爱听？不爱听你也得受着！」

「你这下贱的脏线轴婊子！」

「婊子才在你姐的棺材里绕线轴呢。」

她们是西印度群岛来的姑娘。

「我叫俺兄弟来，替你把那屁股揍个够。」
===Original===
“Gash it and stop you’ jawing.”

They were interrupted by another West Indian girl, who wore a pink-flowered muslin frock and a wide jippi-jappa hat from which charmingly hung two long ends of broad pea-green ribbon.

“It’s a shame. Can’t you act like decent English people?” she said. Gently she began pushing away the assaulted girl, who burst into tears.

“She come boxing me up ovah a dutty-black ’Merican coon.”

“Mek a quick move or I’ll box you bumbole ovah de moon,” her assailant cried after her.…
===Chinese===
「嘎，闭上你那张嚼舌的嘴。」

另一个西印度姑娘过来打断了她们。她穿一件粉花细布连衣裙，戴一顶宽檐吉皮雅帕（jippi-jappa）草帽，帽子上妩媚地垂着两条宽宽的豆绿色缎带。

「真不害臊。你们就不能像体面的英国人那样行事？」她说着，轻轻地把挨打的姑娘推开，那姑娘放声哭了。

「就为个脏黑的黑鬼美国佬，她就上手捶我。」

「你再不麻溜儿挪窝，我一拳把你那屁眼儿抡到月亮上去，」动手的姑娘冲着她的背影喊。……
===Original===
“The monkey-chasers am scrapping,” Zeddy commented.

“In a language all their own,” said Jake.

“They are wild womens, buddy, and it’s a wild language they’re using, too,” remarked a young West Indian behind Jake.

“Hmm! but theyse got the excitement fever,” a lemon-colored girl at a near table made her contribution and rocked and twisted herself coquettishly at Jake.…
===Chinese===
「追猴子的娘们儿开打啦，」泽迪评了一句。

「用的还是他们自家独一份儿的语言，」杰克说。

「她们是野娘们儿，兄弟，用的也是一门野语言，」杰克背后一个年轻的西印度人搭腔。

「哼！可她们这是热昏发烧，」邻桌一个柠檬色的姑娘也凑上一句，冲着杰克卖俏地摇身扭腰。……
===Original===
Susy had already reached the pavement with Miss Curdy and Strawberry Lips. Susy breathed heavily.

“Lesh git furthest away from this low-down vice hole,” she said. “Leave that plug-ugly nigger theah, I ain’t got no more use foh him nohow.”

“I never did have any time for Harlem,” said Miss Curdy. “When I was high up in society all respectable colored people lived in Washington. There was no Harlem full a niggers then. I declare—”
===Chinese===
苏西已经同柯迪小姐和草莓唇到了人行道上。苏西喘着粗气。

「咱们快离这下作的罪恶窝子远远的，」她说。「那丑八怪黑鬼就撂在那儿吧，反正他这人我再没半点用处了。」

「我从来就没瞧上过哈莱姆，」柯迪小姐说。「想当年我在上流社会高处的时候，有头有脸的有色人全住在华盛顿。那时候还没有这么个塞满黑鬼的哈莱姆。我说呀——」
===Original===
“I should think the nigger heaven of a theater downtown is better than anything in this heah Harlem,” said Susy. “When we feels like going out, it’s better we enjoy ourse’f in the li’l corner the white folks ’low us, and then shuffle along back home. It’s good and quiet ovah in Brooklyn.”

“And we can have all the inside fun we need,” said Miss Curdy.

“Brooklyn ain’t no better than Harlem,” said Strawberry Lips, running the words rolling off his tongue. “Theah’s as much shooting-up and cut-up in Prince Street and—”

“There ain’t no compahrison atall,” stoutly maintained Susy. “This here Harlem is a stinking sink of iniquity. Nigger hell! That’s what it is. Looka that theah ugly black nigger loving up a scrimpy brown gal right bef oh mah eyes. Jest daring me to turn raw and loose lak them monkey-chasing womens thisanight. But that I wouldn’t do. I ain’t a woman abandoned to sich publicity stunts. Not even though mah craw was full to bursting. Lemme see’m tonight.… Yessam, this heah Harlem is sure nigger hell. Take me way away from it.”
===Chinese===
「要我说，市里戏园子的黑鬼天堂（顶层楼座），也比这哈莱姆里的什么玩意儿都强，」苏西说。「咱们想出门散心的时候，还是在白佬赏给咱们的小角落里乐和乐和，然后一步三摇地挪回窝去。布鲁克林那边儿，妥妥的清净。」

「再说，咱们要的乐子，关起门来也尽够啦，」柯迪小姐说。

「布鲁克林不比哈莱姆强，」草莓唇说，字字在舌尖上打着滚儿溜出来。「王子街（Prince Street）上，开枪的、动刀的一样少不了，还有——」

「根本没法儿比，」苏西斩钉截铁地咬定。「这哈莱姆就是个臭烘烘的罪恶大泥坑。黑鬼地狱！就是这玩意儿。瞧瞧那边那个丑黑鬼，当着我的眼皮子，就跟一个干瘦的棕皮小妞亲热上了。存心激我，好叫我今晚也学那帮追猴子的娘们儿撒泼放野。可那种事我才不干。我可不是个能干出这种当众把戏的女人。哪怕我这口气把嗉子撑得炸开也不干。让我今晚就瞧着吧。……是啦，太太，这哈莱姆千真万确就是黑鬼地狱。带我离这儿，越远越好。」
===Original===
---

When Zeddy at last said good night to his newfound brown, he went straight to an all-night barrelhouse and bought a half a pint of whisky. He guzzled the liquor and smashed the flask on the pavement. Drew up his pants, tightened his belt and growled, “Now I’m ready for Susy.”

He caught the subway train for Brooklyn. Only local trains were running and it was quite an hour and a half before he got home. He staggered down Myrtle Avenue well primed with the powerful stimulation of gin-and-whisky.

At the door of Susy’s apartment he was met by his suitcase. He recoiled as from a blow struck at his face. Immediately he became sober. His eyes caught a little white tag attached to the handle. Examining it by the faint gaslight he read, in Susy’s handwriting: “Kip owt that meen you.”

Susy had put all Zeddy’s belongings into the suitcase, keeping back what she had given him: two fancy-colored silk shirts, silk handkerchiefs, a mauve dressing-gown, and a box of silk socks.
===Chinese===
---

泽迪终于跟他新相中的棕皮妞儿道了晚安，径直奔了一家通宵营业的小酒馆，买了半品脱威士忌。他把酒一口气灌下肚，把酒瓶在人行道上砸了个粉碎。提一提裤腰，勒紧皮带，吼了一声：「这下我可以对付苏西了。」

他赶上去布鲁克林的地铁。那会儿只剩慢车在跑，足足花了一个半钟头他才到家。他跌跌撞撞走下默特尔大道，杜松子酒兑威士忌的那股猛劲儿，把他烧得正旺。

苏西公寓的门口，迎着他的竟是他的手提箱。他像脸上挨了一拳似的往后一仰。酒立时就醒了。他瞥见箱把上拴着一张小白牌。就着昏昏的煤气灯光细看，上面是苏西的手笔：「Kip owt that meen you.」——「待在外头，说的就是你。」

苏西把泽迪的全部家当都装进了箱子，单单扣下她送他的东西：两件花梢的绸衬衫，绸手帕，一件淡紫色晨衣，一盒丝袜。
===Original===
“What he’s got on that black back of his’n he can have,” she had said while throwing the things in the bag.

Zeddy beat on the door with his fists.

“Wha moh you want?” Susy’s voice bawled from within. “Ain’tchu got all you stuff theah? Gwan back where youse coming from.”
===Chinese===
「他黑脊梁上正穿着的那一身，就赏他好啦，」她往袋子里扔东西的时候说。

泽迪抡起拳头砸门。

「你还要咋的？」苏西的声音在里头吼。「你的东西不全给你撂那儿了？打哪儿来的滚回哪儿去。」
===Original===
“Lemme in and quit you joking,” cried Zeddy.

“You ugly flat-footed zigaboo,” shouted Susy, “may I ketch the ’lectric chair without conversion ef I ’low you dirty black pusson in mah place again. And you better git quick foh I staht mah dawg bawking at you.”

Zeddy picked up his suitcase. “Come on, Mistah Bag. Le’s tail along back to Harlem. Leave black woman ’lone wif her gin and ugly mug. Black woman is hard luck.”
===Chinese===
「放我进去，别闹笑话了，」泽迪喊。

「你这丑八怪扁脚板的黑鬼子，」苏西吼道，「我要是再放你这脏黑鬼进我的门，就叫我上电椅、临死连忏悔都捞不着。趁我还没放出狗来冲你叫，赶紧滚。」

泽迪提起手提箱。「走哇，箱子先生。咱们一路拖回哈莱姆去。让黑女人守着她的杜松子酒和她的丑脸盘子吧。黑女人，就是灾星。」

## VIII The Raid of the Baltimore / VIII 突袭「巴尔的摩」

===Original===
The blazing lights of the Baltimore were put out and the entrance was padlocked. Fifth Avenue and Lenox talked about nothing else. Buddy meeting buddy and chippie greeting chippie, asked: “Did you hear the news?”… “Well, what do you know about that?”

Yet nothing sensational had happened in the Baltimore. The police had not, on a certain night, swept into it and closed it up because of indecent doings. No. It was an indirect raid. Oh, and that made the gossip toothier! For the Baltimore was not just an ordinary cabaret. It had mortgages and policies in the best of the speakeasy places of the Belt. And the mass of Harlem held the Baltimore in high respect because (it was rumored and believed) it was protected by Tammany Hall.

Jake, since he had given up hoping about his lost brown, had stopped haunting the Baltimore, yet he had happened to be very much in on the affair that cost the Baltimore its license. Jake’s living with Rose had, in spite of himself, projected him into a more elegant atmosphere of worldliness. Through Rose and her associates he had gained access to buffet flats and private rendezvous apartments that were called “nifty.”
===Chinese===
「巴尔的摩」夜总会那耀眼的灯火熄灭了，大门口上了挂锁。第五大道和勒诺克斯大道上，人们别的不谈，光谈这一件事。兄弟碰上兄弟，小妞遇上小妞，开口就问：“听说了吗？”……“嗬，你说这事儿怪不怪？”

可「巴尔的摩」里头压根没出什么耸人听闻的事。警察并没有在某个夜里因为伤风败俗的勾当冲进去把它封掉。不是的。那是一场间接的突袭。哦，这么一来，闲话越发有嚼头了！因为「巴尔的摩」可不是一家普普通通的卡巴莱（cabaret）酒馆。它在黑人区顶气派的那些地下酒吧里，押着契据、握着保单。而哈莱姆大众对「巴尔的摩」敬重非常，因为（流言这么传，大家也这么信）它有坦慕尼协会（Tammany Hall）撑腰。

杰克自从对找回他那失落的棕肤姑娘断了念想，就不再泡「巴尔的摩」了，可偏偏，「巴尔的摩」丢掉执照的那桩事，他卷得很深。杰克跟罗丝（Rose）同居，身不由己地把他推进了一层更雅致的浮华天地。经罗丝和她那伙人引路，他进得了一批号称“顶呱呱”的私酒公寓和私人幽会寓所。
===Original===
And Jake was a high favorite wherever he went. There was something so naturally beautiful about his presence that everybody liked and desired him. Buddies, on the slightest provocation, were ready to fight for him, and the girls liked to make an argument around him.

Jake had gained admission to Madame Adeline Suarez’s buffet flat, which was indeed a great feat. He was the first longshoreman, colored or white, to tread that magnificent red carpet. Madame Suarez catered to sporty colored persons of consequence only and certain groups of downtown whites that used to frequent Harlem in the good old pre-prohibition days.

“Ain’t got no time for cheap-no-’count niggers,” Madame Suarez often said. “Gimme their room to their company any time, even if they’ve got money to spend,” Madame Suarez came from Florida and she claimed Cuban descent through her father. By her claim to that exotic blood she moved like a queen among the blue-veins of the colored sporting world.

But Jake’s rough charm could conquer anything.
===Chinese===
杰克走到哪儿都是顶招人待见的。他这个人天生带着一种说不出的好看，人人都喜欢他，人人也巴望他。弟兄们为他，芝麻大点儿的由头就肯拔拳相向；姑娘们也爱围着他争来吵去。

杰克得以跨进阿德琳·苏亚雷斯夫人（Madame Adeline Suarez）的私酒公寓，实在是一桩了不起的功业。他是头一个踏上那匹华贵红地毯的码头工人——不管黑的白的。苏亚雷斯夫人只招待有头有脸的黑人玩主，外加下城来的某几路白人客——禁酒令以前的美好老日子里，这路人常来哈莱姆。

“便宜没斤两的黑鬼，我可没工夫搭理，”苏亚雷斯夫人常说。“哪怕他们带着钱来花，我也是宁肯屋里空着，也不要他们这号人陪坐，”苏亚雷斯夫人是佛罗里达来的人家，自称父亲那头有古巴血统。凭这一身异邦血统的名头，她在黑人浪荡世界里那些“青筋隐现”的浅肤色贵族中间，走动得像个女王。

可杰克那股粗野的迷人劲儿，什么都能征服。
===Original===
“Ofay’s mixing in!” he exclaimed to himself the first night he penetrated into Madame Suarez’s. “But ofay or ofay not, this here is the real stuff,” he reflected. And so many nights he absented himself from the Congo (he had no interest in Rose’s art of flirting money out of hypnotized newcomers) to luxuriate with charmingly painted pansies among the colored cushions and under the soft, shaded lights of Madame Suarez’s speakeasy. It was a new world for Jake and he took it easily. That was his natural way, wherever he went, whatever new people he met. It had helped him over many a bad crossing at Brest at Havre and in London.… Take it easy… take life easy. Sometimes he was disgusted with life, but he was never frightened of it.

Jake had never seen colored women so carefully elegant as these rich-browns and yellow-creams at Madame Suarez’s. They were fascinating in soft bright draperies and pretty pumps and they drank liquor with a fetching graceful abandon. Gin and whisky seemed to lose their barbaric punch in that atmosphere and take on a romantic color. The women’s coiffure was arranged in different striking styles and their arms and necks and breasts tinted to emphasize the peculiar richness of each skin. One girl, who was the favorite of Madame Suarez, and the darkest in the group, looked like a breathing statue of burnished bronze. With their arresting poses and gestures, their deep shining painted eyes, they resembled the wonderfully beautiful pictures of women of ancient Egypt.

Here Jake brushed against big men of the colored sporting world and their white friends. That strange un-American world where colored meets and mingles freely and naturally with white in amusement basements, buffet flats, poker establishments. Sometimes there were two or three white women, who attracted attention because they were white and strange to Harlem, but they appeared like faded carnations among those burning orchids of a tropical race.
===Chinese===
“白佬掺和进来了！”头一晚钻进苏亚雷斯夫人家的时候，他对自己惊叹了一句。“可管他白佬不白佬，这儿这才是真货色，”他寻思着。于是多少个夜晚，他撇下「刚果」（the Congo）不去（罗丝那套从被迷得晕头转向的生客手里勾钱的把戏，他毫无兴趣），跑到苏亚雷斯夫人这地下酒吧的彩色软垫中间、柔和的灯影底下，同那些妆点得娇媚动人的脂粉客一起受用。对杰克来说，这是个新世界，而他受得从容自在。他天生就是这份做派，走到哪儿，碰上什么生人，都是如此。多少回险恶的横渡——布雷斯特（Brest）、勒阿弗尔（Havre）、伦敦——都靠它撑了过去。……慢慢来……日子慢慢过。有时候他对生活腻烦透了，可他从来没有怕过它。

杰克从来没见过像苏亚雷斯夫人家这些深棕油亮、奶油黄嫩姑娘们这样精心雅致的黑人女子。她们裹着柔软鲜亮的绸缎，蹬着漂亮的浅口高跟鞋，喝酒喝得那样放浪又那样优雅，撩人心弦。杜松子酒和威士忌在这气氛里仿佛褪去了粗野的烈劲，染上了一层浪漫色彩。女人们的发式各各梳得醒目，手臂、脖颈和胸脯都敷了色，好把各自肤色那份独特的丰润衬托出来。有一个姑娘是苏亚雷斯夫人的心头肉，一群人里数她最黑，看上去就像一尊会呼吸的擦亮青铜雕像。她们那些引人注目的姿态和手势，那深亮亮的描过的眼睛，活像古埃及女子画像里那种美妙绝伦的形影。

在这儿，杰克同黑人浪荡世界的大人物们及其白人朋友摩肩碰面。那是一个怪异的、不美国的世界：在游乐地下室里、私酒公寓里、扑克赌场里，有色人种同白人自由而自然地相遇相混。偶尔有两三个白种女人，因为她们是白人、在哈莱姆显得稀罕，很招人注目，可她们立在那些热带人种灼灼如火的兰花中间，就像蔫了的康乃馨。
===Original===
One night Jake noticed three young white men, clean-shaven, flashily-dressed, who paid for champagne for everybody in the flat. They were introduced by a perfectly groomed dark-brown man, a close friend of the boss of the Baltimore. Money seemed worthless to them except as a means of getting fun out of it. Madame Suarez made special efforts to please them. Showed them all of the buffet flat, even her own bedroom. One of them, very freckled and red-haired, sat down to the piano and jazzed out popular songs. The trio radiated friendliness all around them. Danced with the colored beauties and made lively conversation with the men. They were gay and recklessly spendthrift.…

They returned on a Saturday night, between midnight and morning, when the atmosphere of Madame Suarez’s was fairly bacchic and jazz music was snake-wriggling in and out and around everything and forcing everybody into amatory states and attitudes. The three young white men had two others with them. At the piano a girl curiously made up in mauve was rendering the greatest ragtime song of the day. Broadway was wild about it and Harlem was crazy. All America jazzed to it, and it was already world-famous. Already being jazzed perhaps in Paris and Cairo, Shanghai, Honolulu, and Java. It was a song about cocktails and cherries. Like this in some ways:

> Take a juicy cocktail cherry,
 

 Take a dainty little bite,
 

 And we’ll all be very merry
 

 On a cherry drunk tonight.
> We’ll all be merry when you have a cherry,
 

 And we’ll twine and twine like a fruitful vine,
 

 Grape vine, red wine, babe mine, bite a berry,
 

 You taste a cherry and twine, rose vine, sweet wine.
 

 Cherry‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee, cherry‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee, ee‑ee‑ee
 

 Cherry‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee, cherry‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee‑ee, ee‑ee‑ee
 

 Grape vine, rose vine, sweet wine.…

> Love is like a cocktail cherry,
 

 Just a fruity little bit,
 

 And you’ve never yet been merry,
 

 If you’ve not been drunk on it.
> We’ll all be merry when you have.…
===Chinese===
一天夜里，杰克注意到三个年轻白人，胡子刮得精光，衣裳穿得花哨，满公寓的香槟都归他们付账。引他们进来的是个拾掇得一丝不苟的深棕色男子，「巴尔的摩」老板的密友。钱在他们眼里仿佛不值一钱，不过是捞乐子的手段。苏亚雷斯夫人格外卖力地讨他们欢心，把整座私酒公寓带他们看遍，连她自己的卧房也开了门。他们当中的一个，满脸雀斑、一头红发，坐到钢琴跟前，把流行歌曲爵士了一番。这三位向四下里辐射着亲热劲儿，跟有色美人跳舞，同男人们谈笑风生。他们又快活又挥金如土。……

一个星期六夜里，半夜与天亮之间，他们又来了，还多带了两个。这当口，苏亚雷斯夫人家里的气氛简直是酒神当道，爵士乐像蛇一样扭进扭出、缠上一切，把人人都逼进情动的状态和姿态里去。钢琴前一个姑娘妆化得古里古怪，一身淡紫，正唱着当今顶红的拉格泰姆歌曲。百老汇为它疯了，哈莱姆为它狂了。全美国都踩着它爵士，它早已名满天下。巴黎、开罗、上海、檀香山、爪哇，说不定也早爵士上它了。那是一支关于鸡尾酒和樱桃的歌。有几分像这样：

> 摘一颗汁水汪汪的鸡尾酒樱桃，
 

 小口小口咬一咬，
 

 咱们个个乐陶陶，
 

 今晚就醉这一场樱桃倒。
> 你拿一颗樱桃来，大家乐一遭，
 

 咱们像多子的藤儿缠啊绕，
 

 葡萄藤，红酒暖，宝贝心肝，咬颗莓，
 

 你尝颗樱桃缠一缠，蔷薇藤，甜酒甜。
 

 樱桃——咿——咿——咿——咿，樱桃——咿——咿——咿——咿——咿，咿——咿——咿
 

 樱桃——咿——咿——咿——咿，樱桃——咿——咿——咿——咿——咿，咿——咿——咿
 

 葡萄藤，蔷薇藤，甜酒甜。……

> 爱情就像鸡尾酒樱桃，
 

 就那么一口果子的量，
 

 你要是没醉倒在它身上，
 

 就从来没有乐过一场。
> 你拿一颗樱桃来，大家乐一遭。……
===Original===
The women, carried away by the sheer rhythm of delight, had risen above their commercial instincts (a common trait of Negroes in emotional states) and abandoned themselves to pure voluptuous jazzing. They were gorgeous animals swaying there through the dance, punctuating it with marks of warm physical excitement. The atmosphere was charged with intensity and overcharged with currents of personal reaction.…
Then the five young white men unmasked as the Vice Squad and killed the thing.
Dicks! They had wooed and lured and solicited for their trade. For two weeks they had spilled money like water at the Baltimore. Sometimes they were accompanied by white girls who swilled enormous quantities of champagne and outshrieked the little ginned-up Negresses and mulattresses of the cabaret. They had posed as good fellows, regular guys, looking for a good time only in the Black Belt. They were wearied of the pleasures of the big white world, wanted something new—the primitive joy of Harlem.
So at last, with their spendthrift and charming ways, they had convinced the wary boss of the Baltimore that they were fine fellows. The boss was a fine fellow himself, who loved life and various forms of fun and had no morals about them. And so one night when the trio had left their hired white ladies behind, he was persuaded to give his youthful white guests an introduction to Madame Adeline Suarez’s buffet flat.….

---
===Chinese===
女人们被那纯粹的欢愉节奏卷走了，连做买卖的本能也抛到了脑后（这是黑人在动情时候的通性），一头栽进纯粹肉感的爵士摇摆里。她们是华美的动物，在舞步里摇曳，时不时迸出几记温热的肉体激动，给这舞打上标点。空气里绷足了烈度，人与人感应的电流满屋乱窜，早已过了量。……

跟着，那五个年轻白人摘下面具——原来是风化缉查队（Vice Squad），欢宴当场毙命。

密探！他们原来一直在为自己的差事献殷勤、下饵子、钓罪证。整整两个星期，他们在「巴尔的摩」花钱像淌水。有时还带着白种姑娘，那些姑娘灌香槟灌得海量，尖叫起来压过卡巴莱里那些杜松子酒灌起来的黑人小妞和混血娘们儿。他们装成好哥们儿、地道的小伙子，一心只在黑人区（Black Belt）寻乐子。白色大世界的享乐他们腻了，想要点新鲜——哈莱姆的原始欢愉。

于是到头来，凭着这副挥金如土又讨人喜欢的做派，他们到底让「巴尔的摩」那提防心极重的老板信了：他们都是上等好人。老板自己也是个上等好人，热爱生活，热爱五花八门的乐子，对这些乐子毫无道德芥蒂。于是一天夜里，趁着那三位没带他们雇来的白种女人，老板被说动了，把他那几位年轻白客引荐给了阿德琳·苏亚雷斯夫人的私酒公寓。……

---
===Original===
The uniformed police were summoned. Madame Suarez and her clients were ordered to get ready to go down to the Night Court. The women asked permission to veil themselves. Many windows were raked up in the block and heads craned forth to watch the prisoners bundled into waiting taxicabs. The women were afraid. Some of them were false grass-widows whose husbands were working somewhere. Some of them were church members. Perhaps one could claim a place in local society!
They were all fined. But Madame Suarez, besides being fined, was sent to Blackwell’s Island for six months.
To the two white girls that were also taken in the raid the judge remarked that it was a pity he had no power to order them whipped. For whipping was the only punishment he considered suitable for white women who dishonored their race by associating with colored persons.
The high point of the case was the indictment of the boss of the Baltimore as accessory to the speakeasy crime. The boss was not convicted, but the Baltimore was ordered to be padlocked. That decision was appealed. But the cabaret remained padlocked. A black member of Tammany had no chances against the Moral Arm of the city.
The Belt’s cabaret sets licked their lips over the sensation for weeks. For a long time Negro proprietors would not admit white customers into their cabarets and near-white members of the black race, whose features were unfamiliar in Harlem, had a difficult time proving their identity.
===Chinese===
制服警察奉召来了。苏亚雷斯夫人和她的客人们被勒令准备好，下楼去过夜间法庭（Night Court）。女人们请求准许蒙上面纱。整条街上好多扇窗户哗啦啦推起来，一颗颗脑袋探出张望，看囚犯们被塞进候在那儿的出租汽车。女人们怕极了。她们有些是名不副实的“活寡妇”，口称丈夫在什么地方做活。有些是教堂会众。说不定哪一位，在本地社会上还占着一个座次呢！

他们一律罚了款。苏亚雷斯夫人除了罚款，还被发落到布莱克韦尔岛（Blackwell’s Island）去，关了六个月。

对同样在这次查抄中被拿住的两个白种姑娘，法官发了话：可惜他无权下令抽她们鞭子。因为在他看来，那些同有色人种厮混、辱没了自家种族的白种女人，唯有鞭刑才算相称的惩罚。

这案子的高潮，是「巴尔的摩」老板被控为地下酒吧罪的从犯。老板没有定罪，可「巴尔的摩」被下令挂锁查封。裁定提起了上诉。可那家卡巴莱依旧锁着。坦慕尼协会里一名黑人会员，对上这座城市的“道德臂膀”（Moral Arm），毫无胜算。

一连好几个星期，黑人区的卡巴莱圈子咂着嘴唇，把这桩耸动的新闻品了又品。好长一段日子，黑人老板不让白人顾客进自家的卡巴莱，而黑人里那些近白肤色、面孔在哈莱姆眼生的成员，得费好大的劲才能证明自己的身份。

## IX Jake Makes a Move / IX 杰克行动了

===Original===
Coming home from work one afternoon, Jake remarked a taxicab just driving away from his house. He was quite a block off, but he thought it was his number. When he entered Rose’s room he immediately detected an unfamiliar smell. He had an uncanny sharp nose for strange smells. Rose always had visitors, of course. Girls, and fellows, too, of her circle. But Jake had a feeling that his nose had scented something foreign to Harlem. The room was close with tobacco smoke; there were many Melachrino butts in a tray, and a half-used box of the same cigarettes on a little table drawn up against the scarlet-covered couch. Also, there was a half-filled bottle of Jake’s Scotch whisky on the table and glasses for two. Rose was standing before the dresser, arranging her hair.

“Been having company?” Jake asked, carelessly.

“Yep. It was only Gertie Blake.”
===Chinese===
一天下午，杰克下工回家，正瞅见一辆出租汽车从他住的那所房子跟前开走。他人还隔着足足一个街区，但他寻思那车停的正是他的门牌。他一走进罗丝（Rose）的房间，立刻嗅出一股生疏的气味。他生着一只对怪味儿灵得出奇的鼻子。罗丝当然向来有客来访。她那个圈子里的姑娘们，也有小伙子。可杰克总觉得，自己的鼻子嗅到了一缕哈莱姆地面上没有的味儿。屋里让烟熏得发闷；烟灰碟里堆满了梅拉克里诺（Melachrino）烟蒂，猩红罩面的长沙发旁拉着一张小桌，桌上搁着吃剩半盒的同样的香烟。桌上还有半瓶杰克自己的苏格兰威士忌，摆着两只杯子。罗丝正站在梳妆台前，拢着她的头发。

“家里来过客人吧？”杰克满不在乎地问。

“可不。就格蒂·布莱克（Gertie Blake）一个。”
===Original===
Jake knew that Rose was lying. Her visitor had not been Gertie Blake. It had been a man, a strange man, doubtless a white man. Yet he hadn’t the slightest feeling of jealousy or anger, whatever the visitor was. Rose had her friends of both sexes and was quite free in her ways. At the Congo she sat and drank and flirted with many fellows. That was a part of her business. She got more tips that way, and the extra personal bargains that gave her the means to maintain her style of living. All her lovers had always accepted her living entirely free. For that made it possible for her to keep them living carefree and sweet.

Rose was disappointed in Jake. She had wanted him to live in the usual sweet way, to be brutal and beat her up a little, and take away her money from her. Once she had a rough leather-brown man who used to beat her up regularly. Sometimes she was beaten so badly she had to stay indoors for days, and to her visiting girl pals she exhibited her bruises and blackened eyes with pride.

As Jake was not brutally domineering, she cooled off from him perceptibly. But she could not make him change. She confided to her friends that he was “good loving but” (making use of a contraction that common people employ) “a big Ah-Ah all the same.” She felt no thrill about the business when her lover was not interested in her earnings.

Jake did not care. He did not love her, had never felt any deep desire for her. He had gone to live with her simply because she had asked him when he was in a fever mood for a steady mate. There was nothing about Rose that touched and roused him as his vivid recollection of his charming little brown-skin of the Baltimore. Rose’s room to him was like any ordinary lodging in Harlem. While the room of his little lost brown lived in his mind a highly magnified affair: a bed of gold, fresh, white linen, a magic carpet, all bathed in the rarest perfume.﻿… Rose’s perfume made his nose itch. It was rank.
===Chinese===
杰克心里明白，罗丝在扯谎。来的客不是格蒂·布莱克。来的是个男人，一个生人，十有八九是个白人。然而无论来的是谁，他心里竟没泛起一星半点的妒意或火气。罗丝男朋友女朋友都有，行事向来放得开。在「刚果」（the Congo）夜总会，她陪人坐着、喝着，跟许许多多小伙子打情骂俏。那是她营生的一部分。这样小费挣得多，还有额外的私人交易，供她维持那份生活的排场。她那些相好的，向来都认下她这份全然自在的活法。因为正是这样，她才养得起他们，叫他们过得无忧无虑、甜甜蜜蜜。

罗丝对杰克是失望的。她本想叫他照老例那种甜蜜法子过活：凶一凶，时不时揍她一顿，把她的钱从她手里夺走。从前她有过一个粗野的汉子，一身皮革似的棕色，揍她揍得极有规律。有时揍得太狠，她只得一连几天闭门不出；可等上门来的姐妹们到了，她会满心得意地把青一块紫一块的伤痕和乌黑的眼圈亮给她们看。

杰克偏不逞凶称霸，她对他的情分就看得见地凉了下去。可她没法叫他改样儿。她跟姐妹们交心说，他“亲热是好亲热，可”（她用了市井人家惯说的一种省略话）“到底还是个大糊涂蛋”。相好的对她的进项不上心，她觉得这桩营生就没有了滋味。

杰克满不在乎。他不爱她，对她也从没起过什么深切的欲念。当初搬来跟她住，不过是因为他正犯着渴求一个固定相好的热症，又赶巧是她开口相求。罗丝身上没有一样东西碰得到他、撩得起他，比不得他心头那个鲜灵灵的记忆——“巴尔的摩”夜总会那个迷人的小棕肤宝贝。罗丝的房间在他眼里，不过是哈莱姆一处置寻常的落脚处。而他那走失了的棕色小宝贝的房间，在他心里却是一桩放大了又放大的仙居：一张金床，浆洗一新的雪白亚麻布，一张魔毯，通身浸在最稀罕的香水里。……罗丝的香水呛得他鼻子发痒。那股味儿，太冲了。
===Original===
He came home another afternoon and found her with a bright batik kimono carelessly wrapped around her and stretched full-length on the couch. There were Melachrino stubs lying about and his bottle of Scotch was on the mantelpiece. Evidently the strange visitor of the week before had been there again.

“Hello!” She yawned and flicked off her cigarette ash and continued smoking. A chic veneer over a hard, restless, insensitive body. Fascinating, nevertheless.﻿… For the moment, just as she was, she was desirable and provoked responses in him. He shuffled up to the couch and caressed her.

“Leave me alone, I’m tired,” she snarled.

The rebuff hurt Jake. “You slut!” he cried. He went over to the mantelpiece and added, “Youse just everybody’s teaser.”
===Chinese===
又过几天，一个下午他回到家，只见她披一件鲜亮的蜡染和服，松松垮垮裹着身子，直挺挺躺在长沙发上。满地滚着梅拉克里诺烟蒂，他那瓶苏格兰威士忌搁在壁炉台上。不用说，上礼拜那个生客又来过了。

“哈罗！”她打个哈欠，弹掉烟灰，接着抽她的烟。一层俏丽的外壳，罩着一副硬实、躁动、麻木不仁的身板儿。然而照样迷人。……就这一刻，就她眼下这副样子，她是撩人的，在他身上勾起了回应。他拖着步子蹭到沙发边，抚弄起她来。

“别缠我，人家乏了，”她恶声恶气地呛了一句。

这一鼻子灰扎疼了杰克。“你个骚货！”他嚷道。他走到壁炉台跟前，又补上一句：“你如今是谁都要逗一把的公共玩意儿。”
===Original===
“You got a nearve talking to me that way,” said Rose. “Since when you staht riding the high horse?”

“It don’t take no nearve foh me to tell you what you is. Fact is I’m right now sure tiahd to death of living with you.”

“You poor black stiff!” Rose cried. And she leaped over at Jake and scratched at his face.

Jake gave her two savage slaps full in her face and she dropped moaning at his feet.

“There! You done begged foh it,” he said. He stepped over her and went out.

Walking down the street, he looked at his palms. “Ahm shame o’ you, hands,” he murmured. “Mah mother useter tell me, ‘Nevah hit no woman,’ but that hussy jest made me do it﻿… jest *made* me.﻿… Well, I’d better pull outa that there mud-hole.﻿… It wasn’t what I come back to Gawd’s own country foh. No, sirree! You bet it wasn’t.﻿…”
---
===Chinese===
“你倒有胆子这样跟我说话，”罗丝说。“打几时起，你也昂首挺胸骑上高头大马了？”

“要说破你是个什么货色，我还用不着多大的胆子。实打实讲，这会儿我真是腻味透顶，再也没法儿跟你过下去了。”

“你这可怜的黑木头疙瘩！”罗丝尖叫。她朝杰克扑将过去，一把抓挠他的脸。

杰克劈头盖脸给了她两记野蛮的耳光，她呻吟着瘫倒在他脚前。

“瞧见没！这是你自个儿讨来的，”他说。他跨过她的身子，出门去了。

顺着街往前走，他盯住自己两只手掌看。“我替你们害臊，手啊，”他咕哝着。“我妈从前老念叨：‘打死也甭打女人’，可那骚婆娘愣是逼得我下了手……愣是*逼*的。……唉，我还是趁早从那个烂泥坑里拔脚吧。……我回到上帝自家的国度来，可不是图这个。才不是呢，千真万确！你只管放心，不是。……”
---
===Original===
When he returned to the house he heard laughter in the room. Gertie Blake was there and Rose was telling a happy tale. He stood by the closed door and listened for a while.

“Have another drink, Gertie. Don’t ever get a wee bit delicate when youse with me.﻿… My, mah dear, but he did slap the daylights outa me. When I comed to I wanted to kiss his feet, but he was gone.”

“Rose! You’re the limit. But didn’t it hurt awful?”

“Didn’t hurt enough. Honey, it’s the first time I ever felt his real strength. A hefty-looking one like him, always acting so nice and proper. I almost thought he was getting sissy. But he’s a *ma‑an* all right.﻿…”
===Chinese===
他回到那所房子，听见屋里笑声朗朗。格蒂·布莱克在那儿，罗丝正讲着一桩乐事。他站在掩关的门外，听了一会儿。

“再来一杯，格蒂。跟我在一块儿，千万别来那份儿小家子气的斯文。……哎哟我的妈，亲爱的，他那一顿耳光，真把我打得灵魂出了窍。等我缓醒过来，恨不得去亲他的脚，可他早没影儿了。”

“罗丝！你可真没治了。就难道不疼得钻心吗？”

“还不够疼呢。宝贝儿，这可是我头一回掂出他真力气的斤两。那么壮实的一条大汉，行事又向来那么和和气气、规规矩矩，我差点儿当他是要变成个娘娘腔了。谁知道他可是个地道的老*爷*们儿，错不了。……”
===Original===
A nasty smile stole into Jake’s features. He could not face those women. He left the house again. He strolled down to Dixie Red’s poolroom and played awhile. From there he went with Zeddy to Uncle Doc’s saloon.

He went home again and found Rose stunning in a new cloth-of-gold frock shining with brilliants. She was refixing a large artificial yellow rose to the side of a pearl-beaded green turban. Jake, without saying a word, went to the closet and took down his suitcase. Then he began tossing shirts, underwear, collars, and ties on to the couch.

“What the devil you’re doing?” Rose wheeled round and stared at him in amazement, both hands gripping the dresser behind her.

“Kain’t you see?” Jake replied.
===Chinese===
一丝下流的笑意偷偷爬上杰克的嘴角。他没法儿跟那两个女人照面。他又出了门。他遛达到迪克西·雷德（Dixie Red）台球房，打了一会儿球。打那儿，他又跟泽迪一道去了「老杜克」酒馆。

他又回了一趟家，只见罗丝一身新的金线织锦连衣裙，饰钻闪闪，美艳得晃眼。她正把一朵大大的假黄玫瑰重新别到钉珠的绿头巾侧面。杰克一声不吭，走到衣橱跟前取下提箱，动手把衬衫、内衣、硬领、领带一样样甩到长沙发上。

“你这是撞着哪门子鬼了？”罗丝一个急转身，惊愕地瞪着他，两手死死扒住身后的梳妆台。

“你没长眼睛？”杰克答道。
===Original===
She moved down on him like a panther, swinging her hips in a wonderful, rhythmical motion. She sprang upon his neck and brought him down.

“Oh, honey, you ain’t mad at me ’counta the little fuss tonight?”

“I don’t like hitting no womens,” returned Jake’s hard-breathing muffled voice.

“Daddy! I love you the more for that.”

“You’ll spile you’ new clothes,” Jake said, desperately.

“Hell with them! I love mah daddy moh’n anything. And mah daddy loves me, don’t he? Daddy!”
---
===Chinese===
她像一头母豹似的朝他逼近，胯骨摆出妙不可言的节律。她一纵身攀上他的脖颈，把他扑倒在地。

“哦，宝贝儿，你不是为今晚那点儿小别扭跟我怄气吧？”

“我不稀罕动手打女人，”杰克喘着粗气的闷嗓子回答。

“爹地！就冲这个我更疼你了。”

“你会糟蹋了你那身新衣裳，”杰克说，已是绝望的口气。

“衣裳叫它见鬼去！我疼我的爹地，疼过一切。我爹地也疼我，对不对？爹地！”
---
===Original===
Rose switched on the light and looked at her watch.

“My stars, daddy! We been honey-dreaming some! I am two hours late.”

She jumped up and jig-stepped. “I should worry if the Congo﻿… I should worry mumbo-jumbo.”
===Chinese===
罗丝啪的一声拧亮电灯，看了看手表。

“我的老天，爹地！咱们这场甜梦做得可够久的！我迟到了两个钟头啦。”

她一蹦而起，踢踏着舞步。“「刚果」嘛，我才不愁呢……愁什么呀，装神弄鬼。”
===Original===
She smoothed out her frock, arranged her hair, and put the turban on. “Come along to the Congo a little later,” she said to Jake. “Let’s celebrate on champagne.”

The door closed on him.﻿…

“O Lawdy!” he yawned, stretched himself, and got up. He took the rest of his clothes out of the closet, picked up the crumpled things from the couch, packed, and walked out with his suitcase.
===Chinese===
她抚平连衣裙，拢好头发，戴上头巾。“待会儿跟我一块儿上「刚果」去，”她对杰克说。“咱们喝香槟庆祝庆祝。”

门合上了，把他关在了屋里。……

“哦，老天爷！”他打个哈欠，伸个懒腰，爬起来。他把剩下的衣物从衣橱里取出来，捡起沙发上揉皱的东西，打点停当，拎着提箱走了出去。

## Second Part / 第二部

## X The Railroad / X 铁路

===Original===
Over the heart of the vast gray Pennsylvania country the huge black animal snorted and roared, with sounding rods and couplings, pulling a long chain of dull-brown boxes packed with people and things, trailing on the blue-cold air its white masses of breath.

Hell was playing in the hot square hole of a pantry and the coffin-shaped kitchen of the dining-car. The short, stout, hard-and-horny chef was terrible as a rhinoceros. Against the second, third, and fourth cooks he bellied his way up to the little serving door and glared at the waiters. His tough, aproned front was a challenge to them. In his oily, shining face his big white eyes danced with meanness. All the waiters had squeezed into the pantry at once, excitedly snatching, dropping and breaking things.

“Hey, you there! You mule!”¹ The chef shouted at the fourth waiter. “Who told you to snitch that theah lamb chops outa the hole?”

“I done think they was the one I ordered—”

===Chinese===
在宾夕法尼亚灰茫茫的辽阔腹地上空，那头庞大的黑色动物喷着鼻息、吼叫着，连杆与车钩哐啷作响，拖着一长串暗褐色的匣子，里面塞满了人和物件，把一团团白茫茫的呵气，拖曳在青蓝冰冷的天光里。

地狱正在餐车里开演——在那滚烫的方形配膳口，在那口棺材似的厨房里。那又矮又壮、皮硬茧厚的厨师长，凶得像头犀牛。他挺着肚皮，一路逼近二厨、三厨和四厨，挤到小小的配膳门前，狠狠瞪着侍者们。他系着围裙的结实前胸，就是向他们下的战书。在他那张油光锃亮的脸上，一双大白眼珠子翻跳着刻薄。所有的侍者一齐挤进配膳间，手忙脚乱地抓抢，摔的摔，砸的砸。

「喂，说你呢！你这个骡子！」¹厨师长冲着第四侍者大吼。「谁叫你从洞口顺走那几块羊排的？」

「俺寻思这该是俺点下的那份——」

===Original===
“Done think some hell, you down-home black fool. Ain’t no thinking to be done on here—”

“Chef, ain’t them chops ready yet?” a waiter asked.

“Don’t rush me, nigger,” the chef bellowed back. “Wha’ yu’all trying to do? Run me up a tree? Kain’t run this here chef up no tree. Jump off ef you kain’t ride him.” His eyes gleamed with grim humor. “Jump off or lay down. This heah white man’s train service ain’t no nigger picnic.”

The second cook passed up a platter of chops. The chef rushed it through the hole and licked his fingers.

“There you is, yaller. Take it away. Why ain’t you gone yet? Show me some service, yaller, show me some service.” He rocked his thick, tough body sideways in a sort of dance, licked the sweat from his brow with his forefinger and grunted with aggressive self-satisfaction. Then he bellied his way back to the range and sent the third cook up to the serving window.

===Chinese===
「寻思个鬼！你这乡下土黑傻瓜。这儿轮不着谁寻思——」

「厨师长，那些羊排还没弄好么？」一个侍者问。

「别催俺，黑鬼，」厨师长吼了回去。「你们这帮人想干啥？撵俺上树？没人能把俺这个厨师长撵上树。骑不上他，就趁早跳下来。」他眼里闪着阴森森的幽默。「要么跳下来，要么趴下。这趟白佬的火车差事，可不是黑鬼的野餐会。」

二厨递上来一盘羊排。厨师长一把从洞口塞出去，舔了舔手指头。

「给你，黄皮。端走。咋还不滚？麻利点干活，黄皮，麻利点干活。」他把那厚实坚硬的身躯左右摇摆，像在跳一种舞，用食指抹去眉毛上的汗，哼哧着，那股自鸣得意劲儿咄咄逼人。随后他又挺着肚皮挪回炉灶跟前，把三厨支到配膳窗口去。

===Original===
“Tha’s the stuff to hand them niggers,” he told the third cook. “Keep ’em up a tree all the time, but don’t let ’em get you up there.”

Jake, for he was the third cook, took his place by the window and handed out the orders. It was his first job on the railroad, but from the first day he managed his part perfectly. He rubbed smoothly along with the waiters by remaining himself and not trying to imitate the chef nor taking his malicious advice.

Jake had taken the job on the railroad just to break the hold that Harlem had upon him. When he quitted Rose he felt that he ought to get right out of the atmosphere of Harlem. If I don’t git away from it for a while, it’ll sure git me, he mused. But not ship-and-port-town life again, I done had enough a that here and ovah there. … So he had picked the railroad. One or two nights a week in Harlem. And all the days on the road. He would go on like that until he grew tired of that rhythm. …

===Chinese===
「给那帮黑鬼就得这么给，」他对三厨说。「叫他们时时刻刻趴在树上，可别叫他们把你撵到那上头去。」

杰克——三厨就是他——在窗口站定了位置，把点的菜一样样递出去。这是他头一回在铁路上找活儿，可打头一天起，他那一摊就料理得妥妥帖帖。他同侍者们处得顺顺溜溜，靠的是本色自己，既不去学厨师长的样儿，也不听他那套歹毒的馊主意。

杰克上铁路干活，就为的是挣断哈莱姆箍在他身上的那股劲儿。撇下罗丝（Rose）那会儿，他就觉得应当马上走出哈莱姆的空气。俺要是不躲开它一阵子，它准会把俺逮住，他寻思。可决不再过轮船跟港口城那号日子，这边也好，海外那边也好，俺都受够了。……于是他挑了铁路。一礼拜在哈莱姆过一两夜，其余的日子全在路上。他就这么过下去，直到过腻了这种节奏为止。……

===Original===
The rush was over. Everything was quiet The corridors of the dining-car were emptied of their jam of hungry, impatient guests. The “mule” had scrubbed the slats of the pantry and set them up to dry. The other waiters had put away silver and glasses and soiled linen. The steward at his end of the car was going over the checks. Even the kitchen work was finished and the four cooks had left their coffin for the good air of the dining-room. They sat apart from the dining-room boys. The two grades, cooks and waiters, never chummed together, except for gambling. Some of the waiters were very haughty. There were certain light-skinned ones who went walking with pals of their complexion only in the stopover cities. Others, among the older men, were always dignified. They were fathers of families, their wives moved in some sphere of Harlem society, and their movements were sometimes chronicled in the local Negro newspapers.

Sitting at one of the large tables, four of the waiters were playing poker. Jake wanted to join them, but he had no money. One waiter sat alone at a small table. He was reading. He was of average size, slim, a smooth pure ebony with straight features and a suggestion of whiskers. Jake shuffled up to him and asked him for the loan of two dollars. He got it and went to play. …

---

===Chinese===
忙劲过去了。一切归于安静。餐车的过道里，那些饿着肚子、等得不耐烦的宾客挤成的人堆散空了。「骡子」把配膳间的格栅板擦洗干净，竖起来晾着。别的侍者把银器、杯子和用脏的台布都收好了。膳务员在车厢自己那一头核对着账单。连灶上的活也收了尾，四个厨子走出他们的棺材，到餐室的好空气里透气。他们同餐室的侍生们分开坐。厨子与侍者这两个等级，除了赌钱，从不厮混。有些侍者傲气得很。有那么几个浅肤色的，只在中途停靠的城市里，才同自己这种肤色的伙伴一道上街遛达。另一些上了年纪的，则永远一派庄重。他们是有家室的人，太太们在哈莱姆社会的某个圈子里走动，他们的行止有时还会登上本地黑人报纸。

四个侍者围着一张大桌子打扑克。杰克想入伙，可他囊中空空。一个侍者独自坐在小桌旁看书。他中等个儿，精瘦，一身滑润的纯乌木色，五官端正，腮边隐约有点胡髭。杰克拖着步子蹭过去，问他借两块钱。借到了，便玩牌去了。……

---

===Original===
Jake finished playing with five dollars. He repaid the waiter and said: “Youse a good sport. I’ll always look out for you in that theah hole.”

The waiter smiled. He was very friendly. Jake half-sprawled over the table. “Wha’s this here stuff you reading? Looks lak Greek to me.” He spelled the title, “*S*-*A*-*P*-*H*-*O*, *Sapho*.”

“What’s it all about?” Jake demanded, flattening down the book on the table with his friendly paw. The waiter was reading the scene between Fanny and Jean when the lover discovers the letters of his mistress’s former woman friend and exclaims: “Ah *Oui… Sapho… toute la lyre.*…”

===Chinese===
杰克收场时赢了五块钱。他还了侍者的钱，说：「你这家伙够义气。往后在那个洞口，俺总归照应你。」

侍者微微一笑。他待人很亲热。杰克半个身子趴在桌上。「你念的这是啥玩意儿？俺瞅着就像希腊文。」他一个字母一个字母拼那书名：「*S*-*A*-*P*-*H*-*O*，《萨福》（*Sapho*）。」

「这书讲的啥？」杰克追问，用他那只亲热的大手把书按平在桌上。侍者正读到法妮（Fanny）与让（Jean）之间的一场戏：那做情人的发现了情妇旧日女友的书信，失声惊叹：「Ah *oui… Sapho… toute la lyre*（啊，是的……萨福……整架里拉琴都为她鸣响）……」

===Original===
“It’s a story,” he told Jake, “by a French writer named Alphonse Daudet. It’s about a sporting woman who was beautiful like a rose and had the soul of a wandering cat. Her lovers called her Sapho. I like the story, but I hate the use of Sapho for its title.”

“Why does you?” Jake asked.

“Because Sappho was a real person. A wonderful woman, a great Greek poet—”

“So theah *is* some Greek in the book!” said Jake.

The waiter smiled. “In a sense, yes.”

===Chinese===
「是一个法国作家写的故事，」他对杰克说，「作家名叫阿尔丰斯·都德（Alphonse Daudet）。写的是一个风尘女子，人美得像朵玫瑰，魂儿却是只浪荡猫。她的情人们管她叫萨福。故事我喜欢，可我讨厌拿『萨福』来作书名。」

「你为啥讨厌？」杰克问。

「因为萨福（Sappho）是真有其人的。一位了不起的女人，一位伟大的希腊诗人——」

「敢情这书里头还真有希腊文！」杰克说。

侍者微微一笑。「就某种意思说，是的。」

===Original===
And he told Jake the story of Sappho, of her poetry, of her loves and her passion for the beautiful boy, Phaon. And of her leaping into the sea from the Leucadian cliff because of her love for him.

“Her story gave two lovely words to modern language,” said the waiter.

“Which one them?” asked Jake.

“Sapphic and Lesbian… beautiful words.”

“What is that there Leshbian?”

“… Lovely word, eh?”

===Chinese===
于是他给杰克讲起萨福的故事：讲她的诗，讲她的几段情爱和她对美貌少年法翁（Phaon）的痴恋；还讲她因为爱他，从琉卡狄亚的悬崖纵身跳进了大海。

「她的故事给近代语言留下了两个可爱的词，」侍者说。

「哪两个？」杰克问。

「一个是 Sapphic（萨福体的），一个是 Lesbian（莱斯博斯的）……两个美好的词。」

「那个『莱斯比安』是个啥？」

「……多美的词儿，是吧？」

===Original===
“Tha’s what we calls bulldyker in Harlem,” drawled Jake. “Them’s all ugly womens.”

“Not *all*. And that’s a damned ugly name,” the waiter said. “Harlem is too savage about some things. *Bulldyker*,” the waiter stressed with a sneer.

Jake grinned. “But tha’s what they is, ain’t it?”

He began humming:

> “And there is two things in Harlem I don’t understan’
> It is a bulldyking woman and a faggoty man.…”

Charmingly, like a child that does not know its letters, Jake turned the pages of the novel. …

===Chinese===
「这号人，哈莱姆俺们管她叫男人婆（bulldyker），」杰克拖长了声调说。「那号女人，个个都是丑婆娘。」

「不见得*个个*都丑。再说这名字也忒难听了，」侍者说。「哈莱姆在有些事儿上太粗野。男人婆（*Bulldyker*），」侍者带着讥诮，把字咬得重重的。

杰克咧嘴一笑。「可她们本来就是这个样，不是么？」

他哼哼起来：

> 「哈莱姆有两样事儿俺闹不明白：
> 一样是男人婆式的女人，一样是娘娘腔的男人。……」

杰克一页一页翻着那本小说，憨态可掬，像个还不认得字母的娃娃。……

===Original===
“Bumbole! This heah language is most different from how they talk it.”

“Bumbole” was now a popular expletive for Jake, replacing such expressions as “Bull,” and “blimey.” Ever since the night at the Congo when he heard the fighting West Indian girl cry, “I’ll slap you bumbole,” he had always used the word. When his friends asked him what it meant, he grinned and said, “Ask the monks.”

“You know French?” the waiter asked.

===Chinese===
「邦波（Bumbole）！这上头印的语言，跟他们嘴里说的差得可老远了。」

「邦波」如今成了杰克爱用的骂语，顶替了从前那些「胡扯」「见鬼」之类的惊叹。自从在「刚果」的那一夜，他听见那个好斗的西印度群岛姑娘尖声叫嚷「俺抽你这个邦波」，他就一直用这个词。朋友们问他是什么意思，他咧嘴一笑：「去问和尚。」

「你懂法语？」侍者问。

===Original===
“*Parlee-vous? Mademoiselle, un baiser, s’il vous plait. Voilà!* I larned that much offn the froggies.”

“So you were over there?”

“*Au oui, camarade*,” Jake beamed. “I was way, way ovah there after Democracy and them boches, and when I couldn’t find one or the other, I jest turned mah black moon from the A.E.F. … But you! How come you jest plowing through this here stuff lak that? I could nevah see no light at all in them print, chappie. *Eh bien. Mais vous compris beaucoup.*”

“*C’est ma langue maternelle.*”

“Hm!” Jake made a face and scratched his head. “*Comprendre pas*, chappie. Tell me in straight United States.”

===Chinese===
「*Parlee-vous? Mademoiselle, un baiser, s’il vous plait. Voilà!*（会讲法语么？小姐，赏个吻，劳驾。瞧好！）就会这么几句，还是跟那帮法国佬捡来的。」

「这么说，你也在那边待过？」

「*Au oui, camarade*（是喽，同志），」杰克满面红光。「俺老远老远跑过大洋那边，去追民主，去追那帮德国佬；到头来两样一样也寻不着，俺就把黑屁股一扭，开小差脱离了美国远征军（A.E.F.）……可你呢！你怎么能把这种玩意儿囫囵个儿啃得下去？那些印出来的字儿，俺从来一丝儿亮也瞅不见，老伙计。*Eh bien. Mais vous compris beaucoup*（呃，好家伙。你可真懂得多）。」

「*C’est ma langue maternelle*（这是我的母语）。」

「嗯？」杰克做了个鬼脸，挠挠头皮。「*Comprendre pas*（俺听不懂），老伙计。跟俺说地道的美利坚话吧。」

===Original===
“French is my native language. I—”

“Don’t crap me,” Jake interrupted. “Ain’tchu—ain’tchu one of us, too?”

“Of course I’m Negro,” the waiter said, “but I was born in Haiti and the language down there is French.”

“Haiti… Haiti,” repeated Jake. “Tha’s where now? Tha’s—”

“An island in the Caribbean—near the Panama Canal.”

===Chinese===
「法语是我的母语。我——」

「别糊弄俺，」杰克打断他。「你不是——你不是也是俺们自己人？」

「我当然是黑人，」侍者说，「可我生在海地（Haiti），那边通行的语言是法语。」

「海地……海地，」杰克念叨着。「那地在哪儿来着？那——」

「加勒比海上的一座岛——挨着巴拿马运河。」

===Original===
Jake sat like a big eager boy and learned many facts about Haiti before the train reached Pittsburgh. He learned that the universal spirit of the French Revolution had reached and lifted up the slaves far away in that remote island; that Black Haiti’s independence was more dramatic and picturesque than the United States’ independence and that it was a strange, almost unimaginable eruption of the beautiful ideas of the “*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité*” of Mankind, that shook the foundations of that romantic era.

For the first time he heard the name Toussaint L’Ouverture, the black slave and leader of the Haitian slaves. Heard how he fought and conquered the slave-owners and then protected them; decreed laws for Haiti that held more of human wisdom and nobility than the Code Napoleon; defended his baby revolution against the Spanish and the English vultures; defeated Napoleon’s punitive expedition; and how tragically he was captured by a civilized trick, taken to France, and sent by Napoleon to die brokenhearted in a cold dungeon.

“A black man! A black man! Oh, I wish I’d been a soldier under sich a man!” Jake said, simply.

===Chinese===
杰克像个求知若渴的大孩子似的坐着，火车到匹兹堡以前，他学到了许多关于海地的事。他得知法国大革命那股普世的精神，曾远播到那座天涯孤岛，把那里的奴隶托举了起来；得知黑海地的独立，比合众国的独立更富戏剧性、更多姿多彩；得知那是人类「*Liberté, Egalité, Fraternité*（自由、平等、博爱）」的美好理念一次奇异的、几乎无从想象的迸发，撼动过那个浪漫时代的根基。

他头一回听见杜桑·卢维杜尔（Toussaint L’Ouverture）这个名字——一个黑奴，海地奴隶众人的领袖。听见他如何奋战、降服了奴隶主，随后又保护了他们；如何为海地颁定的法令，比《拿破仑法典》含着更多的人间智慧与高贵；如何捍卫他襁褓中的革命，抵御西班牙和英国那两帮秃鹫；如何击溃拿破仑的惩戒远征军；又是如何悲惨地中了文明人的奸计被擒，被解往法国，由拿破仑发落，在一座阴冷的地牢里忧伤而死。

「一个黑人！一个黑人！唉，俺要是当过这号人手底下的兵该有多好！」杰克朴实地说。

===Original===
